# Armature — Session History

The log of AI-assisted sessions, one entry per session, newest first. `SESSION.md` holds the state a session needs; this file holds why things are as they are, and is read only when `SESSION.md` or a What's Next item points here, or when no ADR or document answers a "why". Written on 2026-10-30 from the three records every session had left in `SESSION.md` until then (its Current Phase paragraph, its What's Done section and its Recent Sessions bullets), kept word for word under one heading per session, with that session's "facts worth carrying forward" and the decisions it recorded. From here on, `prompts/update-session.md` appends one entry per session.

**The arc in one line, as `SESSION.md` kept it:** Schema loaded → Seed data inserted → Demo API documented → Next.js scaffolded → GET endpoints live → Types generator implemented → POST /courses + POST /modules live → CoQui fit analysis (ADRs 0016–0023) → Development plan, TerminusDB verification, docs vendoring, research survey → Phase 0 hygiene, CI, ADR-0026 → ADR-0054 Hono → Phase 1 schema catch-up → ADR-0055, ADR-0025, Phase 2 version control → ADR-0032, generic writes, invariants engine, legacy retired → ADR-0029 coverage algorithm, intelligence reads, ADR-0056 nothing derived is stored, Coverage View → documentation overhaul and the explainer for learning scientists → ADR-0057 `asOf`, branch-route race, list filters and the non-aggregation guard, ADR-0058 item statistics as metrics, ADR-0007 confirmed → ADR-0028 Attestation, part-level notes and findings, client changelog, three research mappings, precedents and plugin survey → ADR-0060 unaligned items, ADR-0061 formative use of items in activities (October 2026) → CI timeouts, ADR-0062 operator tools with the commit marker and the seed check in CI, the seed's content gaps, the item-statistics read, the module-content read (2026-10-22)

## 2026-11-09, night (PRs #122 to #127 merged; S1, S6 and the release-path findings R4 to R9, S11; the third rehearsal; `desktop-v0.1.0` tagged, signed and drafted)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the desktop program's step 5 through its release: `desktop-v0.1.0` tagged on `main` at `3318043`, built on four runners, signed under the `release` environment after Amos's approval, and a complete draft release awaiting publication (2026-11-09 night).** The evening's two pull requests merged (#122, #123), the banner's identifiers renamed (#124), then the review's S1 and S6 (#125) and its release-path findings R4, R5, R7, R8, R9 and S11 (#126), R6 recorded as fixed by the two-job workflow. The third rehearsal (`desktop-rehearsal-3` at `4c6d8e1`) ran green and was installed on the Mac, connected to officepi; the first `desktop-v0.1.0` run failed on Windows before building, since the new ancestry check had no `shell: bash` and the rehearsal had skipped it; #127 fixed both and the tag moved to `3318043`. Every signature in the draft's manifest carries the production key's id. Two plans landed from another session in the same checkout (#128 the Storyline bridge revised, #129 a DataShop bridge plan). 20 Rust tests, 24 page tests. **Next: publish the draft (it was still a draft at the close), confirm `desktop-channel` serves its manifest and the installed program answers "the latest version", then `desktop-v0.1.1` for a real update (item 17).**

### Done

- #122 and #123 merged by rebase, CI green on `main` after each; #124 a dated banner line naming the release branch's commits on `main` (`73966cb`, `606c1a5`, `5b6902b`, the acceptance `576ab48`).
- #125, S1: `launch` in `core.rs` clears the sidecar's environment and passes `KEPT_ENVIRONMENT` (the home and temporary folders, the three OneDrive variables, `SystemRoot`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`), matched without regard to case, two Rust tests; confirmed on the installed program by `ps -Eww` on `armature-node`: `HOME` and `TMPDIR` alone. S6: the six Node checksums pinned in `prepare.mjs` beside the version, the same-origin `SHASUMS256.txt` fetch gone; a corrupted archive refused and removed. A dated note on ADR-0069 decision 3, the plan, the desktop README, the CI comments, the banner.
- #126, seven commits: R4 (`CHECK_TIMEOUT` 30 s on the builder; the update's inherited timeout cleared and a stall watchdog, `DOWNLOAD_STALL` 60 s, raced against the download; a paused-time test, `tokio` `test-util` as a dev-dependency, `macros` for `select!`); R7 (`login::restart_arguments` drops `--hidden`; the restart on the main thread as Tauri's own does, `cleanup_before_exit` then `process::restart` with the filtered `Env`); R9 (the three caches gone, `-- --locked` to cargo, a `desktop-v*` tag checked against `main` by the compare API, `identical` or `behind`); R5 (`packaged()` by `tauri::utils::platform::bundle_type`, `Found::Packaged`, the watcher says so once, Settings' line, the fake's `?update=packaged`, a browser test); R8 (`login::change`, the entry renewed at every start while wanted, news only when the state changes; the guide's removal section); S11 (`desktop.yml` pinned, `permissions: contents: read`); the banner with R6.
- `desktop-rehearsal-3` on `4c6d8e1`: four builds in 4 to 10 minutes without a cache, the signing job 18 s, the manifest with four platforms; installed on the Mac over the rehearsal-2 build; deleted with its tag by Amos.
- #127: the ancestry step under `shell: bash`, run on every tag and required only of a program tag. `desktop-v0.1.0` moved to `3318043` (Amos deleted the first tag; auto mode refuses tag and release deletion); the run green, the publish job approved by Amos, the draft complete: both macOS disk images and updater archives, the NSIS installer, the AppImage and `.deb`, four `.sig` files, `latest.json` naming four platforms, every signature the production key's (`c1c5ac3f…`, the scratch key `00d51585…`).

### Session notes

- The session opened on the evening's two open pull requests and ran the whole release path: merges, the review's remaining release findings, a rehearsal, the tag. Amos took each item one at a time ("start R4", "commit and start R9"), with the proposed commit shown before each.
- The one defect of the night was mine: a workflow step without `shell: bash` runs under PowerShell on Windows. The rehearsal could not catch it because the step was skipped for rehearsal tags; it now runs on every tag so a rehearsal exercises it.
- A GitHub Actions cache made on a tag is readable only from that tag (and the default branch's caches, whose keys here differ by job id), so the release's three caches could never hit; dropping them cost nothing and removed a restore path into a signed build.
- The compare API answers `identical` or `behind` for `main...<sha>` when the commit is an ancestor of `main`, `ahead` or `diverged` otherwise, and 404 for a commit not on the remote; it works with the read-only job token.
- Another session worked in this same checkout during the night (#128, #129) and left a commit on local `main` that the remote held rebased; `git pull --rebase` dropped it by patch id. Two sessions in one checkout is a risk to the context files; neither touched them.

### Facts from 2026-11-09 night worth keeping

- The updater plugin has one timeout: set on the builder, it bounds the check and is carried in `Update.timeout` (public) into the download as a bound on the whole request; `update.rs`'s header says so, and why the update's copy is cleared.
- `tauri::utils::platform::bundle_type()` is baked in at bundling and is what the plugin uses to pick `linux-x86_64-deb` before `linux-x86_64`; `BundleType` lives in `tauri::utils::config`.
- Tauri's off-main-thread `restart` re-reads the process's own arguments from the managed `Env` at exit and cannot be told others; the main-thread form is `cleanup_before_exit` then `tauri::process::restart(&env)`, both public (`update.rs`).
- A minisign key id is bytes 2 to 10 of the base64 line inside the key or signature file; the configuration's `pubkey` and the manifest's `signature` are each the whole file base64-encoded once more, so two decodes reach it. A check that a draft's signatures are the production key's is a few lines of Python.
- The `rehearsal` environment has no required reviewer, only its tag policy, so a rehearsal run proceeds unattended; `release` requires `mossglenn`.
- The sidecar's environment on a running program is read with `ps -Eww -o command= -p <pid of armature-node>`.

### Decisions taken

- `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` passes to the sidecar, the one Node setting kept, since it adds a certificate authority rather than removing a check and is the way behind an institution's TLS inspection until S7 (`core.rs`'s `KEPT_ENVIRONMENT` comment; the banner). Open to overrule.
- A download is given up on a minute's stall, not after a total time (`update.rs` header; plan §10's Updates note).
- A `.deb` or `.rpm` install is not checked, rather than offered package updates through `pkexec` unrehearsed (plan §10; the guide's table and Updates).
- The release restores no cache; Cargo runs `--locked`; a `desktop-v*` tag must be on `main`; the check runs on every tag and is required only of a program tag (the workflow's header; plan §10's workflow note; the desktop README).
- R10, S2, S3 and S7 wait for 0.1.1 (the banner's line of this night).

### Session notes

- Amos: "go with your proposed sequence", then "commit", "go to the droplet", "commit". The sequence was the review's §"A suggested order" step 1 plus the Droplet run SESSION.md named; D5 and D7 taken as recommended by that approval.
- Each finding's citation was re-read at `7063b4a` before any change; R2 was sharpened by the plugin's source: a non-2xx answer is only logged in its endpoint loop, so one endpoint's failure is indistinguishable from an empty feed, and the fix had to be the shell's.
- The three code commits were split by writing each commit's reduced version of the shared files from the saved finals; a `git reset --soft` after a wrong split left the later changes in the index, caught by `git diff --cached --stat` before the push (memory updated).
- The classifier refused `gh secret set`, a push chained after commits, and once `gh pr create` with a long inline body (a `--body-file` retry passed); the Droplet's first-boot apt lock cost one retry.
- The 2026-11-05 Droplet run had Keycloak on loopback with tokens fetched over SSH; the program's browser sign-in needed it public, hence the third name. The recipe stayed outside the repository, as `docker/sites/README.md`'s mechanism intends.

### Facts from 2026-11-09 evening worth keeping

- **A Tauri `.pub` file's content is the `pubkey` string `tauri.conf.json` takes** (already base64 of the minisign public key file); do not encode it again. Home: `desktop/README.md` names the files; this entry.
- **tauri-plugin-updater 2.14.1 folds every non-2xx feed status into `ReleaseNotFound`** (its endpoint loop logs and moves on). Home: `update.rs`'s header, the plan's §10.
- **Keycloak 26 behind Caddy for a rehearsal:** `KC_HOSTNAME=https://<name>`, `KC_HTTP_ENABLED=true`, `KC_PROXY_HEADERS=xforwarded`; a password-grant token fetched on the Droplet's loopback still carries the public issuer. Home: `deployment.md`, the 2026-11-09 paragraph.
- **The Droplet's timings this time:** SSH about 30 s, apt lock held about a minute by the first-boot upgrade, images 335 s, certificates on the first attempt once the records answered (they already did when Caddy started). Home: `deployment.md`.
- **The Keycloak designer's `sub` on a fresh realm import is not fixed** (`ef2d6d72-…` today, `fcf303f1-…` on the Mac's instance): always decode a token. Home: this entry.
- **`upload-artifact` does not keep a `.app` bundle's symbolic links or modes**, so the build job tars the app itself (`COPYFILE_DISABLE=1`), which is also the updater's artifact. Home: the workflow's comment.

### Session notes

- Amos approved the order proposed at the start (the updater, start at login, icons, the workflow, the documents, the Droplet run) without choosing between one pull request or two and on the Intel build; the defaults were taken, one pull request and Intel kept. No artwork exists, so the icon is a placeholder.
- The updater was rehearsed on the Mac before any release: version 0.1.0 built with a `--config` overlay pointing at a feed on loopback with a scratch key, 0.1.1 signed with it, Amos's click on Install and restart; the hub stopped, the install, the restart into 0.1.1 with one core, then a clean Cmd-Q. Reading the source first found that a restart off the main thread arrives as `ExitRequested`, which the shell takes for a quit, so `exiting` is set before it.

Installed through a browser on the Mac: Gatekeeper's "cannot verify", Open Anyway, then the keychain asking for the login password (Always Allow chosen). On the laptop through Edge from the tailnet: SmartScreen's "Windows protected your PC", More info, Run anyway; a reinstall prompt over the debug build of the same version; no console window; the new icon in the window and tray, the old one on the taskbar (Windows' icon cache by path; no pinned shortcut). The draft and its tag were deleted at the close.
- Step 5's Droplet run was held to the next session (Amos).

### Facts from 2026-11-08 night worth keeping

- `releases/latest/download/latest.json` sees only a published release, never a draft; until one is published the feed is 404 (`docs/desktop-program.md`, `update.rs`'s comment).
- A workflow started by hand must first exist on `main`; a tag runs the workflow file at its own commit, hence rehearsal tags (the workflow's header).
- Tauri passes `TAURI_ENV_TARGET_TRIPLE` (and `_ARCH`, `_PLATFORM`, `_DEBUG`) to `beforeBuildCommand` (`prepare.mjs`).
- macOS ties a keychain item to its maker's designated requirement, a build's own hash under an ad-hoc signature, so an update is expected to ask for the password again; to be seen when `desktop-v0.1.1` installs (plan §15 question 12, evidence note).
- macOS 15 removed right-click Open for an unchecked program; Open Anyway in Privacy & Security is the way (the guide).
- `tauri signer generate` records the version in the signature's trusted comment from CLI 2.12.4, which `requireSignedVersion` checks.

## 2026-11-08, evening (step 4's last rehearsals: the Windows laptop and the Keycloak realm; the run's four findings fixed; PRs #120 and #121)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the desktop program's steps 1 to 4 built and accepted (2026-11-07 and 08; PRs #116 to #121 merged, CI green on `main` at `ee7ef48`).** The core (`app/desktop.ts`, `app/lib/desktop/`) hosts a workspace hub over the file-backed store behind the Tauri shell (`desktop/`), with the PKCE sign-in, the keychain, the tray, the `armature://connect` link and the seven pages. Step 4's rehearsals are done: the Windows laptop against officepi from a debug NSIS installer `desktop.yml` builds when started by hand (PR #120), and the Keycloak realm on the Mac with a remote under `oidc`, whose four findings were fixed and rerun (PR #121): a workspace branch's handle is the email's local part, the Pairing page shows the identifier a tool sends (`toolIdentity`), connecting to a remote under `oidc` drops an identifier kept for `header`, and the name, address and identifier fields opt out of macOS's capitalization. 611 contract tests, 16 page tests, 10 Rust tests. **Next: step 5, the release.**

### Done

- **PR #120** (`ci(desktop)`, `docs`): `desktop.yml` gains `workflow_dispatch` and a `windows` job running only on it (`tauri build --debug --bundles nsis` on `windows-latest`, the artifact `armature-desktop-debug-windows` kept 7 days); the Ubuntu `shell` job skips a dispatched run. Built in 5 min 18 s, 25 MiB. The plan's step 4 note, the README, `desktop/README.md`.
- **The Windows rehearsal** (no repository change but the record): the installer copied by `scp` to `C:\armature-rehearsal\`, checksum matched; installed per user; the link from Win+R, the identifier asked for under `header`, create, a tool's write from the Mac by SSH over the laptop's loopback (403 without the pairing value, 200 with it), push, bring in, deliver, remove; the X hid to the tray; Quit left no process, lock or temporary file. Recorded in the desktop plan's §12.
- **The Keycloak rehearsal:** Keycloak 26.7.5 by `docker compose --profile rehearsal-oidc up -d keycloak`; `armature_oidc` from `load_schema.js --recreate` and `seed_data.js`; the designer's `sub` from a password-grant token; `User/kc-designer` with `issuer`, Administrator and Maintainer by the operator's `put`; the hub bundle, the operator bundle and the Mac's debug app rebuilt from `main` (the hub bundle predated Remove and would have served 2026-11-06, What's Next item 35's case); the program's whole cycle, quit and relaunch signed in from the keychain. Four findings, then a rerun on the rebuilt program that held all four.
- **PR #121**, four commits: (1) `feat(hub)`: `handleFor` in `identity.ts`, the create route building the name after `users.me` (the name's characters checked first, so offline still `bad_name`), the export rewriting either handle, `handle.test.ts` (4 tests), the `oidc` workspace test's owner given an email unlike the `sub`; (2) `feat(desktop)`: the core's `toolIdentity` (`{ user }`, 401 `signed_out` before sign-in), `connect` dropping `user` for an `oidc` remote, the relay's allowlist, the fake core, the Pairing page, their tests; (3) `fix(desktop)`: `verbatim` on seven fields, an attribute check in the page tests (the page-test file staged in two parts); (4) `docs`: the plan's §12 record and step 4 note, dated notes on ADR-0065, ADR-0031 and ADR-0069, `api.md`, a note under `client-changes.md`'s 2026-11-01 entry, the workspace plan, development plan §6, the READMEs' count.

### Session notes

- Started from a clean `main`; Amos chose the hand-started Windows build (option A of three), then the Keycloak rehearsal before recording both in one PR.
- The Keycloak run found the identity model's edge: under `oidc` the `externalId` is an opaque `sub`, which the handle and a tool's `Armature-User` both depended on. Amos asked how stable identity must be (a move between universities, a changed email, what a person may and may not change); the answer laid out what exists (the `User` `@id` as identity, `(issuer, sub)` as the binding, email and institution as attributes, their history a read at a commit) and what does not (several logins for one person, a cross-instance identifier such as ORCID, affiliation at the time of the work), recorded as an open question.
- The F3 check needed Read then Connect on the page; Amos's first "confirmed" had not sent `connect` (no restart in the log, the file's time unchanged), found by the log rather than assumed.
- A "port 3108" read from the screen was the hub's 3101; the log and `settings.json` settled it.

### Facts from 2026-11-08 evening worth keeping

- **A freshly built, ad-hoc-signed macOS bundle takes 6 to 14 s to its listening hub on its first launch, and under a second after** (the spike's 6.1 s on a fresh machine is the same effect). Home: the desktop plan's §5 table already shows it; a relaunch is the measurement to take.
- **A file copied by `scp` carries no mark of the web, so SmartScreen does not show**; a browser's download is still to be seen, at the release (the plan's spike list, note).

- **The designer's Keycloak `sub` is `fcf303f1-4194-485e-b8e7-4e4ca72ca809`**, from `curl -s -d grant_type=password -d client_id=armature-desktop -d username=designer -d password=designer -d scope=openid http://localhost:8080/realms/armature/protocol/openid-connect/token`; Keycloak's development database lives in its container, so a `stop` and `start` keep it.

- **On Windows the program installs to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Armature\` and keeps its data in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\com.mossglenn.armature\`**; quoting PowerShell through `cmd` over SSH breaks, so a `.ps1` is copied and run. Home: memory `tailnet-pc-rehearsal` (updated).

## 2026-11-07 to 08 (TerminusDB confirms the race, #2637; the desktop program's core, shell and pages; Remove, revision 2026-11-07; PRs #116 to #119)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the desktop program's steps 1 to 4 built (2026-11-07 and 08; PRs #116 to #119 merged, CI green on `main` at `0098fda`).** The core (`app/desktop.ts`, `app/lib/desktop/`) hosts a workspace hub over the file-backed store, driven by JSON lines on its standard input and output, with the PKCE sign-in and the credential behind `hub.remoteToken`; the shell (`desktop/`, Tauri 2, installed on its own) runs it as the sidecar `armature-node`, keeps the refresh token and the pairing value in the keychain, and has the tray, the `armature://connect` link and the seven pages of the plan's §4. A workspace can be removed (`DELETE /workspace/:name`, revision 2026-11-07), which the first rehearsal found missing. Rehearsed on the Mac against officepi, which now runs `main` at `0098fda`. 607 contract tests, 15 page tests in Chromium, 10 Rust tests. **Next: §12's remaining rehearsals, the Windows laptop and the Keycloak sign-in, then step 5, the release.**

### Done

- **#116**: TerminusDB's maintainers confirmed the data-version race as a defect on Discord, the fix planned for 12.1.0; filed by Amos as terminusdb/terminusdb#2637 with the repro, run again on v12.0.7 (four writers landing in each of four rounds). ADR-0025 decision 7, note of 2026-11-07; `docs/development-plan.md` §9; check AO's header.
- **#117, the desktop plan's step 3, the core**: `protocol.ts` (requests `{ id, type }`, replies `{ id, status, body }` as a route answers, events `{ event }`, concurrent, the input's end closing it, EPIPE absorbed), `settings.ts`, `folder.ts` (the synced-folder warning), `signIn.ts` (PKCE on an ephemeral loopback port, the issuer's metadata checked, `invalid_scope` answered with `offlineAccess: true`), `credential.ts` (the refresh 30 s before expiry, one in flight, rotation reported, a refused refresh `signed_out`), `core.ts` and `desktop.ts`; `build:desktop` in CI; `core.test.ts` the acceptance (the cycle through the protocol, a push through the credential to an `oidc` remote), `bundle.test.ts` the built core over pipes, which the Windows job runs.
- **#118, step 4a, the shell**: the core's `credential` request after `start` (the shell learns the remote only from `start`'s reply); `desktop/` with Vite, React and Tailwind at Quiver's versions, `scripts/prepare.mjs` (the core's bundle and Node 22.23.4, checksummed); the Rust side (`core.rs`, `relay.rs`, `keychain.rs`, `logs.rs`, `tray.rs`); the deep link, single instance, the tray; `desktop.yml` on Ubuntu (clippy, the Rust tests, a debug `.deb`). Run on the Mac: the hub on 3101 through the sidecar, a link reaching the running program, quit and SIGTERM leaving neither process nor lock; Amos checked the tray, the hidden window and the Dock by eye.
- **#119, step 4b, the pages**: Connect, Sign in, Workspaces, a workspace (push, bring in, deliver, remove), Pairing, Settings, Log; the fake core as a model with `?scenario=`; 15 Playwright paths in `desktop.yml`; the core's `commits` request, the shell's `log_lines`, `pairing_value`, `open_log_folder`. **Remove**: `DELETE /workspace/:name`, a program action, 409 `unpushed_commits`, the remote deleting the branch by its own rule (409 `unmerged_branch` otherwise), `{ workspace, remote: deleted | kept | absent, reason? }`; revision 2026-11-07 (`REVISIONS`, `CLIENT_REVISION`, `workspace.remove`, `client-changes.md`, `api.md`; ADR-0065 decision 2, later change).
- The rehearsal on the Mac against officepi (updated to the branch, then to `main`): connect through the page, create, a tool's write with the pairing value, push, `bring_in_first`, bring in, deliver; then Remove of the delivered workspace, the branch deleted on officepi and its `Owner` grant revoked.

### Session notes

- The core went as planned, three choices made on the session's recommendations; the acceptance tests passed but one, which was the test's own assumption (a warm credential in the same process is not a restart).
- 4a found a real defect on the Mac's bundle: macOS's quit, the Apple Event Cmd-Q sends, ends Tauri's loop with `Exit` and no `ExitRequested`, and waiting there for the stop's reply deadlocked on the main thread the tray needs. Three diagnostic log lines located it; the shell now closes the core's input on `Exit` (ADR-0069 decision 4, note).
- The session recommended putting `desktop/` in the npm workspace, then reversed on finding that both Dockerfiles run `npm ci` from the root lockfile with only `app/` and `packages/` copied, so a workspace member would have changed the images and called for a VM rehearsal.
- The rehearsal found what no test asked: Amos delivered without ticking "delete" and had no way to remove the workspace. Remove was designed (three options), decided as (a), built with its revision and rehearsed the same night.
- The pages' first test run failed 9 of 13: eight were Playwright selectors matching substrings, one a real bug: recent Chromium returns a Promise from `scrollIntoView`, which an arrow-function effect handed React as its cleanup.

### Facts from 2026-11-07 and 08 worth keeping

- macOS's quit gives `RunEvent::Exit` without `ExitRequested`; a handler on `Exit` runs on the main thread, so nothing there may wait on work that needs it (menus, emits) (ADR-0069 decision 4, note; `desktop/src-tauri/src/core.rs`).
- `tauri-plugin-shell`'s `CommandChild` closes the child's input when dropped and kills it only with `kill_on_drop`, off by default (`core.rs`).
- Deep links on macOS reach only a bundled `.app`, the scheme registered from `Info.plist`; `open -n --args` is not how a link arrives there (`desktop/README.md`).
- `keyring` 4 splits the stores into separate crates; 3.6 keeps `Entry::new(service, user)` (`desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml`).
- Recent Chromium's `scrollIntoView` returns a Promise; an effect must not return it (the Log page's commit).
- Playwright's `getByLabel` matches substrings unless `exact`; a region and a field whose labels share words collide.
- After many suite runs `armature_test`'s `main` grows long enough that history, race and merge tests time out at 15 s (six on 2026-11-08); `load_schema.js --recreate` and `seed_data.js` with `TERMINUS_DB=armature_test` clear it (SESSION.md item 20).
- A debug macOS bundle is 149 MiB, the Ubuntu debug `.deb` 101 MiB; `desktop.yml` takes about six minutes with a cold Rust cache.
- A workspace hub's served revision is also the one its client sends its remote, so a revision for a workspace-only route forces every remote to update first (SESSION.md item 35).

### Decisions taken

- The core answers the workspace requests through an in-process second app with the action routes on (ADR-0069, note of 2026-11-07).
- The settings file is the core's; it keeps the remote's identity so the program starts offline (ADR-0069 note; plan §9 and §11).
- The core draws the local token only when none is kept and returns it in `start`'s reply (ADR-0069 note).
- Step 4 as two pull requests; the updater moved to step 5; `desktop/` installed on its own with its own lockfile; the CI debug build on Ubuntu (plan §14).
- Remove, option (a), the remote deleting the branch by its own rule; as revision 2026-11-07 by the rule, remotes updated first (ADR-0065 decision 2, later change of 2026-11-07; `client-changes.md`).
- The write queue stays until a TerminusDB release with the fix is pinned; then check AO runs again and the queue is reconsidered, since it also orders work with no data version (ADR-0025 decision 7, note of 2026-11-07).
- **Names chosen without a separate decision, open to overrule:** the requests `credential`, `commits`, `remove`; the core's codes `not_opened`, `not_started`, `already_started`, `folder_locked`, `port_in_use`, `insecure_remote`, `no_client_id`, `not_a_remote`, `use_connect`, `other_remote`, `not_oidc`, `no_user`, `sign_in_refused`, `sign_in_timeout`, `sign_in_cancelled`, `issuer_unreachable`, `issuer_error`, `signed_out`, `bad_settings`, `workspaces_exist`; Remove's `remote` and `reason` values; the keychain accounts `refresh:<remote>` and `local-token`; keyring 3.6, Node 22.23.4, port 1430 for the pages' dev server; start at login shown and deferred to the release.

## 2026-11-06, night (the local store's drift traced to the suites; test isolation; the per-branch write queue; PRs #114 and #115)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the hub's per-branch write queue built (2026-11-06 night; PR #115 merged, CI green on `main` at `d9ddd40`).** `If-Match` now holds for writes in flight together on one hub: `Store.exclusive`, over `queue.ts`, runs a branch's writes one at a time, from reading the head to the commit, on both stores, with registrations, a branch's creation and deletion, and a merge's landing in it too. A write without `If-Match` that waited its turn and finds a document it replaces changed since it arrived is 409 `document_moved`, so the queue does not undo that promise. A second hub on one store and the operator tools stay outside it (ADR-0025 decision 7, notes of 2026-11-06). The local store's drift is traced to the suites, and `TERMINUS_DB=<name> npm test` now keeps a run off `armature` (PR #114). 545 tests. **Next: the desktop plan's step 3, the core (`phase-8/desktop-core`).**

### Done

- **The local store reseeded** to a fresh seed (71 documents, 2 grants, 6 commits), `check` clean, after comparing it with a fresh seed: its content matched apart from `asOf` commit ids and ordering, plus 162 revoked grants from tests.
- **officepi rebuilt** to `4ef23de`, revision 2026-11-06, in 44 s, the store left running; a write at the current revision passes negotiation, a future one is refused, `check` clean. It holds 72 documents, one past the seed, not looked into. Rebuilt again after the merge to `d9ddd40`, in 42 s, for the queue, its presence checked in the running bundle rather than by concurrent writes, which would have left residue on its `main`.
- **PR #114, two commits, rebase merged.** `envLocal()` in `app/vitest.env.ts` lets a variable set in the shell win, as `loadEnvLocal` does, in the workers and the global setup; `lib/vitestEnv.test.ts`; both READMEs and CLAUDE.md corrected about what a run leaves on `main`; the test count from 296 to 527.
- **PR #115, three commits, rebase merged.** (1) `KeyedQueue` in `queue.ts` and `Store.exclusive` on both stores, the adapter's keyed by database and branch, the file store's beside the queue that orders its commits. (2) The write pipeline from the head read to the commit, a registration, a first encounter's insert, a branch's creation and deletion whole and a merge's last read with its apply all in the queue; the head read as a write arrives, with `refuseOverwrite` shared by both cases; `writeQueue.test.ts`. (3) The ADR note, `api.md`, the explainer, plan §9, CLAUDE.md, the count of 545.

### Session notes

- Amos asked first for the drift and officepi. The survey contradicted the last session's note, so the reseed waited for the cause: `deleteScratchBranch` revokes an `Owner` grant and never deletes it, and `TERMINUS_DB=<scratch> npm test` had been writing to `armature` all along, leaving the scratch database clean, which is probably how the note came to read "leaves two".
- The red baseline for the queue showed more than a lost update: 7 of 9 probes failed, among them two placements at one sequence both landing, a write and a delete both 200, the winner's `Owner` grant revoked by the loser of two creations, and one person as three `User`s.
- Reading `api.md` before writing the docs caught a regression in the first build: a write without `If-Match` that waited its turn read the new head and overwrote the commit ahead of it with a 200, where it had been `document_moved`. A deterministic test holds the queue until both writers have joined, and showed `[200, 200]` before the arrival read.
- Two timeouts of the delete test could have been a deadlock; a raised limit ruled that out, every run finishing in 14 to 44 s on a scratch database with a long log. The cost is the existing `branchHolding` walk, not the queue.
- Claims were corrected along the way: a 404 from a guessed route proved nothing and was rerun, a test's cap of 100 commits failed a count, and a final run that had not run the suite, its log path unset and `| head` hijacked by a shell function of that name, was not reported as a pass.

### Facts from 2026-11-06 night worth keeping

- A fresh seed is 71 documents, 2 grants, 6 commits; 69 is the Design Graph's record count (`app/e2e/graph.spec.ts`), which leaves out the grants. Home: memory (`local-store-drift-from-suites`)
- Every test branch leaves a revoked `Owner` grant and two commits on `main`; a full run about 32 grants and 77 commits; any vitest run without `TERMINUS_DB` leaves one grant from the warm-up, even over a store-free file. Home: `app/README.md`, CLAUDE.md's Tests
- `branchHolding`'s walk makes a delete take seconds on a long log when no branch holds the head. It now holds that branch's queue only. The history test takes 12 to 16 s on such a log against a 15-second limit (item 4)
- `race.test.ts`'s `branch_busy` test failed once in about 42 runs, at 6.2 s, the message lost to a filter; not seen again, in 18 runs with full logs among them. Home: SESSION.md's Blockers
- Vitest's `test.env` is assigned over `process.env`, so a value read from a file there beats the shell unless the reader lets the shell win

### Decisions taken

- **The head read as a write arrives, so `document_moved` survives the queue**, one read per write without `If-Match`: ADR-0025 decision 7, note of 2026-11-06, built. An existing rule kept, not a new decision
- **Queues taken a branch before `main`, a caller resolved before any queue**: the same note, `Store.exclusive`'s comment, CLAUDE.md's What Not To Do
- **Open to overrule:** no limit on the wait; a merge's check and a dry run outside the queue; no client revision, as the behavior the contract already promised
- **A shell variable wins over `.env.local` in the tests** (Amos, on the session's recommendation): PR #114, CLAUDE.md's Tests. Isolation is asked for, not the default

### Carried here from SESSION.md's Active Decisions at this close

- **Choices of 2026-11-06 evening, open to overrule** (PR #113; HISTORY.md, that entry, Decisions taken): `ARMATURE_PUBLIC_URL` read only under `oidc`, since the compose file cannot branch on the resolver; `instance.name` falling back on the public host, then the request's; `/connect` with the navigation label "Your own machine"; `firstEncounterId`, 32 hexadecimal digits of SHA-256 over `[issuer, sub]`

## 2026-11-06, evening (the connection profile and `/connect`; concurrent first sign-ins fixed; `If-Match` found not to hold under concurrency and a write queue decided; PR #113)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the desktop plan's step 2, the hub's side, done (2026-11-06 evening; PR #113 merged, CI green on `main` at `3041578`).** `GET /discover` carries `instance`, the connection profile a person confirms before connecting (revision 2026-11-06), a workspace hub keeping its remote's at the clone; the viewer's `/connect` offers `armature://connect`; the compose file derives `ARMATURE_PUBLIC_URL`, which the hub reads only under `oidc`; the rehearsal realm offers `offline_access`. The registration test PR #85 narrowed found one person becoming several `User`s under concurrent first sign-ins, fixed by an id derived from `(issuer, sub)`, and behind that a wider defect: **`If-Match` does not hold for writes in flight together**, so an update can be lost with every writer told 200 (check AO; ADR-0025 decision 7, note of 2026-11-06). A per-branch write queue in the hub is decided and not built. 523 tests. **Next: item 34, the write queue, then the plan's step 3, the core (`phase-8/desktop-core`).**

### Done

- **PR #113, six commits, rebase merged.** (1) `firstEncounterId` in `identity.ts`, the insert without `If-Match`, the race tests in `oidc.test.ts` (four concurrent first encounters on one `User`, 409 `registration_conflict` after two lost passes, any other store refusal surfaced at once), check AO, ADR-0032 decision 3's note, plan §9 corrected. (2) `instance` in `GET /discover` (`config.ts`'s `instanceName` and `publicUrl`, `version.ts`'s `instanceEntry` and `PROGRAM_RELEASES`, `discovery` now async), `WorkspaceRecord.instance` copied at the clone, `InstanceProfile` and `CLIENT_REVISION` 2026-11-06 in the client, `instance.test.ts`, the revision entry, `api.md`, ADR-0067 decision 9's note. (3) `app/app/connect/page.tsx`, `connectLink` in `viewer.ts`, the navigation link, `connect.spec.ts`. (4) The compose file's two settings, the realm's `offline_access` (an optional client scope, and the role on both people), CI's derivation step, `deployment.md`'s rehearsal record and the provider's paragraph. (5) The plans, the README, the explainer, CLAUDE.md. (6) ADR-0025 decision 7's note, plan §9's mechanism by version, the gap in `api.md`, `write.ts`'s header, item 34.
- **A fresh Lima VM rehearsal** (`armature-profile`), workflow rule 5's: the record is in `docs/deployment.md`, "Rehearsed 2026-11-06". The two realm fixes were applied on the same VM with Keycloak recreated, not on a second fresh VM.
- **A standalone repro** at `~/Dev/armature-handoff/terminusdb-data-version-race.mjs`, run on v12.0.7, v12.0.9 and v12.1.0-rc2; Amos published it as a Gist (https://gist.github.com/mossglenn/53c2d2db87ebdc7fec318face9d10184) and asked TerminusDB's Discord whether the behavior is intended and whether a supported conditional write exists.

### Session notes

- Step 2 turned out smaller and larger than planned: `armature-desktop` and the rehearsal's client id had already landed in PR #108, while the registration test, a small item riding along, exposed a defect in the API's core write contract.
- The chain ran: the test made three `User`s; a probe on a scratch database ruled out the query's missing version and `POST` ignoring the header; concurrent writes at one version all landed; a probe through the hub showed lost updates under `If-Match` in two of five rounds, by timing, which is why no test had caught it. ADR-0032 decision 3 had claimed the opposite since 2026-10-08, unreachable until `oidc`.
- The mechanism was stated first as fact from behavior, then read in the server's source, then corrected: the repro's "0 retries" turned out to be a default on 12.0.x, a real 0 on rc2, and an experiment with retries off showed the retry is the cause on 12.0.x and not on 12.1.
- Amos worked through the problem from first principles (Git's lock, what a retry is for, whether the skipped check is a deliberate tradeoff) before choosing option (a). Their question about the tradeoff surfaced that `If-Match` is coarser than a document, which is a follow-up in item 34.
- The rehearsal's real sign-in, the first run of the plan's flow, was refused twice by the realm, which client credentials and password grants had never shown.

### Facts from 2026-11-06 evening worth keeping

- TerminusDB compares a data version in `before_write`, ahead of `with_transaction`; `retry_transaction` reruns only the body; 12.1's commit queue orders commits after that check. Plan §9 and check AO hold it. Retries at 0 land one writer on 12.0.x and answer the rest 500; `TerminusDB-Transaction-Retry-Count` exists only from 12.1
- Concurrent inserts of one `@id` are settled by the store, one winner and the rest `DocumentIdAlreadyExists` (AO3); nothing else is
- Keycloak refuses the whole request with `invalid_scope` when a client does not offer `offline_access`, and the token exchange with `not_allowed` when the person lacks the role, which imported users do not get (`deployment.md`; plan §6)
- A script logging in to Keycloak over plain HTTP must pass its cookies by hand: Python's cookie jar will not send `Secure` cookies
- The Mac's local `main` holds 197 documents, 128 of them `Grant`s; a full suite run on a fresh seed leaves two, so the suites did not cause it. `TERMINUS_DB=<scratch>` with `load_schema.js --recreate` and `seed_data.js` gives a clean store beside it, and the hub and the viewer read it
  > **Note (2026-11-06, night):** wrong on both counts. The suites did cause it: each test branch leaves a revoked `Owner` grant on `main`. And `TERMINUS_DB=<scratch>` did not reach the tests until PR #114, so the run measured here wrote to `armature`. A fresh seed is 71 documents; 69 is the Design Graph's record count. The 2026-11-06 night entry has the rest.
- `gh run watch --exit-status` returned 0 with five of six jobs in progress on `main`, soon after the merge

### The Current Phase paragraph this close replaced

**Phase 8; the desktop program (the workspace hub plan's step 7) planned, its shape decided and verified, ADR-0069 accepted (PRs #110 to #112 merged, CI green on `main` at `a8a44de`).** The plan is `docs/desktop-program-plan.md`: a Tauri 2 shell for what needs the operating system (windows, tray, keychain, the `armature://` link, updates) over the hub itself on the Node executable the program carries, a sidecar named `armature-node`, the two talking JSON lines over the sidecar's standard input and output, the core stopping when that input closes (ADR-0069). The spike verified it on five setups (the Mac, GitHub's macOS, Windows and Ubuntu runners, the Windows laptop): 0.2 to 1.0 s from launch to a listening hub after the first start, every exit case leaving neither process nor lock, and three platform rules now in ADR-0069 decision 6. The local token is kept in the keychain across the program's starts (ADR-0065 decision 7, note of 2026-11-05). §15's questions 1, 3, 4, 12 and 13 are decided; the rest carry recommendations and none blocks step 2. officepi, a Raspberry Pi 5 on the tailnet, now runs a seeded hub under `header` as a standing test remote. 507 tests. **Next: the plan's §14 step 2, the hub's side (`phase-8/connection-profile`).**

### Session notes

- Amos asked for the pros and cons of Tauri against no shell at all before deciding; the no-shell option (Syncthing's shape) lost the keychain, a visible program, installers and signed updates, and is ADR-0069's second alternative. Question 5 of the first batch was unclear as written and was explained before the decision
- The spike's CI found two of the three platform rules: Windows' `\\?\` prefix, which Node cannot load as its entry script (`EISDIR`), and WebKitGTK hanging under Xvfb until its GPU paths were off. The check script had discarded the shell's output, hiding the Linux cause, and now keeps it
- Driving the laptop: SSH as `amos` works for installs and collection, but no window can be created from an SSH session (WebView2's `Invalid window handle`), and a scheduled task to reach the desktop session was refused by auto mode's classifier, so Amos ran the check at the desk

### Facts from 2026-11-06 worth keeping

- **TerminusDB 12.0.7 needs 4 KB pages:** on a Raspberry Pi 5's default kernel its storage aborts with `<jemalloc>: Unsupported system page size`; `kernel=kernel8.img` under `[all]` in `/boot/firmware/config.txt` fixes it. Node, the hub and a workspace hub over the file-backed store run on 16 KB pages. Home: memory (`officepi-raspberry-pi`); a line in `deployment.md`'s machine section when an ARM instance is documented
- **Raspberry Pi OS's firmware adds `cgroup_disable=memory`;** `cgroup_enable=memory cgroup_memory=1` at the end of `cmdline.txt` overrides it. Home: memory
- **The spike's numbers** (sizes, launch times, memory, the slow first launch on a fresh machine): ADR-0069, Verification, and the plan's §5
- **A rebase merge renames commits, so a commit is cited only after its merge;** a staged `git rm` rides into the next commit. Home: memory (`armature-tooling-facts`)
- **No window over SSH on Windows;** the laptop's SSH is as `amos`, an administrator, the key in `administrators_authorized_keys`. Home: memory (`tailnet-pc-rehearsal`)

## 2026-11-05, evening (the Droplet run under `oidc`, workflow rule 5 before the first remote writing client; Caddy's `ETag` suffix found and fixed, the clone-time guard; PR #109)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; ADR-0067 built but for the desktop program's own sign-in; workflow rule 5's Droplet run for the authentication change done 2026-11-05 evening (PR #109 merged, CI green on `main` at `1d15680`).** A hub under `ARMATURE_IDENTITY=oidc` verifies a bearer token on every request but `GET /discover` and the health probes, resolves a person by `(issuer, sub)`, admits a first encounter by `ARMATURE_OIDC_ADMIT`, resolves a service's token to `agent:<client id>`, takes `Armature-User` beside a service's token only under a `Relay` grant, and is published by Caddy at `api.<site>` under `oidc` only; the viewer holds its own credential; a workspace hub reaches a remote under `oidc` with the owner's token (`ARMATURE_REMOTE_TOKEN`), the remote's `/users/me` checked against the owner (ADR-0067's notes of 2026-11-05; `docs/deployment.md`, "An instance under `oidc`"). The Droplet run proved it from outside through TLS, with a workspace hub on the Mac pushing through the published hub, and found one defect: Caddy's `encode` appended the encoding's name to the hub's `ETag`, so the clone's base was `<commit>-gzip` and the push 412; the hub's site is now served uncompressed, CI asserts it, and a clone refuses such a base at once (502 `remote_error`, `reason: etag_altered`). 507 tests. **Next: the workspace hub plan's step 7, the desktop program (item 17), which starts with a plan of its own.**

### Done

- The Droplet run (`docs/deployment.md`, "An instance under `oidc`", the Droplet paragraph): `armature-demo`, `s-2vcpu-4gb` in `nyc3` (which served the size again), Ubuntu 24.04, the firewall by tag, `root` prep then the `armature` user, `docker/.env` with the Keycloak rehearsal settings and `https`, the images built in 186 s, `--profile hub --profile rehearsal-oidc` first, the schema and the seed through the operator service, the first administrator (the designer, `sub` from a decoded token) by the operator's `put`, then `--profile public` once both `A` records answered at GoDaddy's nameserver; both certificates on the first attempt. From the Mac: 3000, 3100, 6363 and 8080 closed; 308 to `https://` on both names; discovery, both probes, 401 with the Bearer challenge, `signature`, `expired`, the reviewer registered on first encounter with no grants, the viewer's agent `unknown_user` until registered (the viewer 500 until then), the service refused a branch with `credential`, the user list refused to the reviewer and served to the administrator, `kind=agent` open, `Armature-User` beside a person's token `relay`, the designer's branch with its owner grant, a write as the token's person, the reviewer refused on that branch and on `main`, stale `If-Match` 412 and fresh landing, an 11 MB body 413, the operator's `check` clean; a reboot: the API back in 26 s with the stored certificates, the designer's `sub` and grants intact, the pushed document kept, the viewer 500 for under a minute while Keycloak started. Droplet, firewall and `known_hosts` entry deleted; the A records removed by Amos.
- PR #109 (three commits, rebase-merged): `docker/caddy/oidc.caddy` without `encode zstd gzip`, with the reason in its comment, and CI's Caddyfile step asserting the hub's adapted site has no `encode` handler (`jq` over the adapted JSON); `remoteCommit` in `app/lib/api/workspace/remote.ts`, used by `clone.ts` for the base, 502 `remote_error` with `reason: etag_altered` and `commit` before anything is written, the status route reading the remote head from the body; one test in `workspace.test.ts` feeding the clone `abc123-gzip`; `docs/api.md` (the route and the 502 row), `docs/client-changes.md` (riding the 2026-11-05 revision), `docs/deployment.md` (the Droplet paragraph, item 4's proxy sentence, the "check, do not assume" bullet), ADR-0067 decision 7's note of 2026-11-05 (later).
- A workspace hub on the Mac (`app/dist/server.mjs` rebuilt from `main`, workspace mode, `ARMATURE_REMOTE_TOKEN` the designer's five-minute token fetched over SSH) against `https://api.armature.amosglenn.com`: before the fix, created and cloned with base `<commit>-gzip`, local write, push 412 at the remote; after the fix, a fresh workspace cloned with a bare base, the local commit replayed on the Droplet as the designer with `Armature-Workspace-Commit` and `Armature-Authored-At`, read back from the remote; a request as anyone else `remote_identity` both times.
- The Mac's leftovers from the laptop rehearsal stopped: the remote hub on `100.85.125.14:3100` (SIGINT) and the file server on 8000 (SIGTERM; it ignored SIGINT).

### Session notes

- Amos chose Keycloak's rehearsal profile on the Droplet over a real provider, the real domain over `sslip.io` names, and deletion over keeping (the three questions asked before anything billable). The whole Droplet lived about 25 minutes.
- The finding came from the one check the fresh VM could not make: the VM rehearsal's `curl` sent no `Accept-Encoding`, and its responses were under Caddy's 512-byte minimum, so the `ETag` arrived bare there. Node's `fetch` accepts compression, and the export a clone reads is large, so the first real client through the proxy hit it at once. The guide's "check, do not assume" sentence about proxies and `ETag`s, written 2026-10-22, was the right warning.
- Two of the first batch of outside checks were the session's own mistakes (a `DesignNote` without `label`, the list response read as an object) and were rerun; the wrong-audience and wrong-issuer cases were not re-verified on the Droplet (Keycloak's master realm refused the default admin credentials) and rest on the VM rehearsal.
- Amos asked for the clone-time guard the summary had offered as an option; it was built, tested and folded into the same pull request, with the status route's read moved to the body, where a proxy cannot reach.
- Rule 5's fresh-VM rehearsal for a Caddyfile change was not run separately: the change was verified live on the Droplet, which the pull request proposed stands in, and Amos merged on that basis. The rule's text is unchanged.

### Facts from 2026-11-05 (evening) worth keeping

- **Caddy's `encode` appends the encoding's name to a strong `ETag`** (`"<commit>-gzip"`, `-zstd`) on any response it compresses, which is any over 512 bytes whose request accepts it; a weak `ETag` is left alone. Home: `oidc.caddy`'s comment, ADR-0067 decision 7's note, `deployment.md`.
- **The Droplet's timings:** SSH answered about 30 s after creation; the images built in 186 s; certificates 7 and 14 s after Caddy started; the API back 26 s after `reboot`. Home: `deployment.md`, the Droplet paragraph.
- **Keycloak's development database lives in its container**, so a `sub` survives a restart and a reboot, not a recreation of the container; the rehearsal realm's access tokens last 300 s, so a token for a workspace hub is fetched just before use. Home: `deployment.md` (the first), this entry (the second).
- **`docker compose run` and `exec` without `-T` read stdin**, so a script piped to `ssh … bash -s` loses every line after the first such command, silently. Copy the script and run it by path. Home: memory (`armature-tooling-facts`).
- **A `nohup … &` process from a non-interactive shell ignores SIGINT;** `kill -TERM <pid>` stops it, and `kill` on a known pid is allowed where `pkill` is refused. Home: memory.
- **The commit identifier's shape** either store produces is `^[a-z0-9]+$` (TerminusDB 31 characters, the file store 64 hex), now `COMMIT_ID` in `workspace/remote.ts`.

### Decisions taken

- The hub's Caddy site is served uncompressed, rather than the hub emitting weak `ETag`s or tolerating a suffix on `If-Match`: ADR-0067 decision 7, note of 2026-11-05 (later); `docker/caddy/oidc.caddy`; CI's assertion. The viewer's site keeps its `encode`.
- A workspace hub checks a commit identifier it takes from an `ETag` and refuses an altered one at the clone, 502 `remote_error` with `reason: etag_altered` (the code reused with a reason, as `forbidden` carries `reason`), and reads the remote head from a body where one exists: `docs/api.md` (Workspace, the 502 row), `docs/client-changes.md` (2026-11-05, rides the revision).
- The Droplet's live verification stood in for the fresh-VM rehearsal of this Caddyfile change (Amos, by merging PR #109); not a change to the rule.

## 2026-11-05 (the rest of ADR-0067 in four pull requests: the `oidc` resolver, relays and the viewer's credential, the workspace hub's token and the registry's gate, the hub published at `api.<site>` with its rehearsal; PRs #105 to #108)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; ADR-0067 built but for the desktop program's own sign-in, in four pull requests on one day (PRs #105 to #108 merged, CI green on `main` at `31b2f21`).** A hub under `ARMATURE_IDENTITY=oidc` verifies `Authorization: Bearer <token>` with `jose` against the issuer's keys on every request but `GET /discover` and the health probes, resolves a person by `(issuer, sub)`, registers a first encounter only when `ARMATURE_OIDC_ADMIT` admits the token (403 `not_registered` otherwise), resolves a service's token to `agent:<client id>`, and takes `Armature-User` beside a service's token as a relay under a `Relay` grant, the person the author and `Armature-Relayed-By` written (`oidc.ts`, `identity.ts`); `GET /discover` says how to sign in in `identity`; the viewer holds its own credential (`viewerToken.ts`); a workspace hub reaches a remote under `oidc` with the owner's token from its host (`ARMATURE_REMOTE_TOKEN`), the remote's `/users/me` checked against the owner (403 `remote_identity`); the registry (`GET /users`, `GET /documents/User`) is a maintainer's or an administrator's under `oidc`, `profile=full` to anyone with the users its records name (`armature.users`), `kind=agent` open, the mirror of `main` the owner and the agents; the `public` profile publishes the hub at `ARMATURE_API_HOST` under `oidc` only, the guard refusing the bad pairings, with `GET /health/live` and `/ready`, Keycloak in a `rehearsal-oidc` profile, rehearsed on a fresh VM; the operator's `put` takes `User` documents. The Mac's seed was recreated at 848 commits. 506 tests. **Next: the Droplet workflow rule 5 asks for before the first remote writing client, or the workspace hub plan's step 7, the desktop program.**

### Session notes

- The session opened on SESSION.md's pointer to item 3, read ADR-0067, ADR-0068 decision 4 and plan §14, and presented the five choices the notes left open with options and a recommendation, plus a sixth (an issuer for rehearsals); Amos accepted all, and the four pull requests followed in the proposed order, each proposed as a commit sequence, pushed and opened on request, merged by rebase on "confirm and merge", CI confirmed on `main` each time

- The fresh-VM rehearsal of PR 4 found three things fixed on the branch: Keycloak 26 carries `sub` only through its `basic` client scope; the operator's `put` refused `User` documents, so the first administrator under `oidc` could not be written; the viewer kept a refused token after Keycloak was recreated and answered 500 until it expired. A fourth, the Caddy snippet adapting to a catch-all proxy of the hub when the API host is empty, was found by `caddy adapt` before the rehearsal and became the guard's third refusal
- The full suite on the Mac failed four merge and delete cases by timeout and the operator's check (`grant_without_branch`) on the unchanged tree too, at 848 commits on `main`; Amos approved recreating the seed, after which 500 of 500 passed; a record a failing first run of the new operator test left on `main` was removed with the operator's `delete`

### Facts from 2026-11-05 worth keeping

- Keycloak 26's access tokens carry `sub` only through the `basic` client scope, so a realm import that names `defaultClientScopes` explicitly must list `basic` (`docker/rehearsal/keycloak-realm.json`; `docs/deployment.md`); a service account's token has `azp` and no `name`, as the hub's rule expects; `KC_HOSTNAME` sets the issuer, so containers reach the keys and the token endpoint by the service name through the two override variables
- Caddy substitutes `{$VAR}` in an `import` path, which is how one Caddyfile chooses a snippet per resolver; a site block whose address placeholder is empty adapts to a catch-all route, which is why the guard requires a bare `ARMATURE_API_HOST` (ADR-0067 decision 7, note of 2026-11-05)
- Compose interpolates a nested default (`${A:-api.${B:-localhost}}`), used for the API host; `caddy validate` and `caddy adapt` run from the image with the files mounted, which CI's images job now does
- The store's `main` drift reached 848 commits and 500 grants after a day of suite runs (each test branch costs two commits on `main`); the remedy and its threshold are in memory (`armature-tooling-facts`)
- A config factory for `createApp` must build its `memoryRecords()` once, not per request, or a workspace vanishes between calls (the token suite's first failure)
- Seven stopped Lima VMs under `~/.lima` (`armature-oidc` added); a fresh-VM rehearsal with Keycloak took about twenty minutes including two image rebuilds; the rehearsal scripts' pattern (settings, up, load, checks, workspace) is in the deployment guide's section
- The Mac's `nohup` remote for the laptop runs revision 2026-11-03 and would need a rebuild from `main` for any further laptop check; the laptop's rehearsal branch no longer exists

### Decisions taken

- **The five open choices of the `oidc` build** (Amos, on the session's recommendations; ADR-0067 Status note of 2026-11-05 and the notes on decisions 1, 2, 4 and 9): `jose` with its remote key set kept per process by address and the address from the issuer's metadata; the settings read in `config.ts` per request; a token source on `HubConfig` the host supplies for the workspace hub; a service told from a person by the absence of `name`; discovery's `identity` as the resolver, the headers and under `oidc` the issuer, audience and client id
- **Keycloak for rehearsals**, in a profile that ships nowhere by default, over a hosted tenant and CILogon (Amos; ADR-0067 decision 7's note; open question 2 answered for rehearsals only)
- **The registry's gate for a clone, option 3** (Amos; ADR-0068 decision 4, later change of 2026-11-05): `profile=full` to anyone with the users its records name, `armature.users`; `kind=agent` open; the mirror the owner and the agents; `export_full` retired
- **Relays deliver documents only, never beside `Armature-Agent`; every relay refusal one code and reason** (chosen while building; ADR-0067 decision 5's note)
- **The operator's `put` admits `User`** (ADR-0062 decision 8's note of 2026-11-05) and **the viewer forgets a refused token** (ADR-0067 decision 4's note)
- **The seed recreated**, the laptop's rehearsal branch deleted (Amos, 2026-11-05)
- *Moved out of SESSION.md's Active Decisions this session, being settled and already here:* the names chosen 2026-10-31 (the 2026-10-31 entry) and 2026-11-01 (the 2026-11-01 entry, Decisions taken)
- **Names chosen without a separate decision, open to overrule:** `invalid_token` and its reasons, `issuer_unreachable`, `not_ready`, `ARMATURE_OIDC_CLIENT_ID`, `ARMATURE_OIDC_JWKS_URL`, `ARMATURE_HUB_TOKEN`, `ARMATURE_HUB_CLIENT_ID`, `ARMATURE_HUB_CLIENT_SECRET`, `ARMATURE_HUB_TOKEN_ENDPOINT`, `ARMATURE_REMOTE_TOKEN`, `ARMATURE_API_HOST`, `ARMATURE_API_SCHEME`, `KEYCLOAK_HOSTNAME`, `rehearsal-oidc`; `reason: relay` with `relayed`, `reason: remote_identity` with `credential`; `armature.users`; `kind`; `HubConfig.oidc`, `oidc.keys`, `remoteToken`; the `headers` map in discovery's `identity`

## 2026-11-03, late night (item 33's rename retry; step 7's first items, the per-launch secret and the action routes gated; PRs #103 and #104)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; item 33 closed and the workspace hub plan's step 7 begun: the per-launch secret and the gated action routes built on the hub's side (2026-11-03 night; PRs #103 and #104 merged, CI green on `main` at `e1ce076`).** A rename a sync client refuses while it holds the file is tried again through waits of 10 ms doubling to 1.28 s, about 2.5 s in all, then 503 `store_unwritable` naming the file, the temporary removed and the old file standing (the record's §6 option 1, Amos; ADR-0066 Verification, the night's note). Every request to a workspace hub that is not a read carries `Armature-Local-Token`, checked after the loopback boundary and before identity, 403 `forbidden` with `reason: local_token` without it or with another value; a hub whose configuration holds no token refuses every write; the host draws the value and prints it once at start unless `ARMATURE_LOCAL_TOKEN` chose it, so a workspace hub is never open. Push, bring in and deliver are served as routes only under `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_ACTIONS=routes`, which the compose profile and the suites set, and are 403 `forbidden` with `reason: program_action` elsewhere, before the workspace is looked up; `GET /discover` says `localToken: required` and `actions: routes | program`; `@armature/client` has a `localToken` option (ADR-0065 decision 7, the note of 2026-11-03, four choices Amos took on the session's recommendations; ADR-0067 decision 7's note). The compose profile's two settings were rehearsed on a fresh Lima VM. 475 tests. **Next: the laptop's end-to-end check of the rename retry against the kept OneDrive folder, then the `oidc` resolver and what hangs off it (item 3).**

### Done

- **PR #103, two commits** (item 33): `renameInto` in `app/lib/workspaceFolder.ts`, a rename refused with `EPERM`, `EACCES` or `EBUSY` tried again through `RENAME_WAITS`, then the store error kind `unwritable`, which the app answers as 503 `store_unwritable` with `path`, `code`, `attempts` and `Retry-After: 5`; two kept tests reusing the kill test's esbuild child, its `rename` refusing the next head twice (the write lands, no temporary left) and every time (`unwritable` after every wait, the old head whole, a later write lands), both failing against a plain rename; the reference row, the revision entry (riding 2026-11-03), the deployment guide's caution, dated Verification and Consequences notes on ADR-0066, the plan's §10 note, a dated note on the rehearsal record, CLAUDE.md's clause
- **PR #104, four commits** (step 7's first two items): `app/lib/api/localToken.ts` (`requireLocalToken`, `requireActionRoutes`, `drawLocalToken`, `tokenMatches` in constant time); `HubConfig.localToken` and `actionRoutes` from `ARMATURE_LOCAL_TOKEN` and `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_ACTIONS` on a workspace hub only, any other value of the latter refused at start; the middleware after `negotiate` and before `bodyLimit`; the header in the preflight list; discovery's `localToken` and `actions`; `server.ts` drawing and printing the token and saying whether the action routes are served; the three routes gated; `@armature/client`'s `localToken`; `localToken.test.ts` (six cases) and the workspace, cycle, role and boundary suites updated; the compose `workspace` service taking `ARMATURE_LOCAL_TOKEN` from `docker/.env` and setting the actions; the reference (the Identity paragraph, discovery, Workspace, the `forbidden` row), a second revision entry for the date, the deployment guide (the token bullet, the example's header, the fourth rehearsal), the plan's §7, §9 and §14 notes, dated notes on ADR-0065 decision 7 and ADR-0067 decision 7 with their index rows, the client README, the explainer, CLAUDE.md
- **The fourth rehearsal of the `workspace` profile** on a fresh Lima VM `armature-token` (Ubuntu 24.04, Docker 29.1, Compose 2.40): the token drawn and printed, writes refused without it and served with it, reads open, a push landing on the remote; a restart drawing a new token and refusing the old; a chosen token in `docker/.env` honored; the image alone in workspace mode refusing push as `program_action` with `actions: program` and still cloning with the token; the four ports on loopback (`docs/deployment.md`, "A workspace hub on this machine")
- Tests 465 to 475

### Session notes

- The session opened on SESSION.md's pointer to item 33 and presented its three options with the recommendation; Amos took option 1; the fix was built with its two tests confirmed failing against a plain rename before trusting them
- Step 7 began with a read of plan §14, ADR-0065 decision 7's notes and ADR-0067 decision 7's note, and a note of four choices the notes leave open while no desktop program exists, each with options and a recommendation; Amos accepted all four

### Facts from 2026-11-03 (late night) worth keeping

- The token check runs ahead of the remote-only guard, so on a workspace hub a write to `main` without the token answers `local_token`, not `remote_only`; `requireActionRoutes` runs before the workspace lookup, so a probe for `/workspace/<guess>/push` learns nothing about which workspaces exist (code comments in `localToken.ts` and `routes/workspace.ts`)
- esbuild's bundle calls an imported `node:fs/promises` function as a property access at call time, so a child that patches `require('fs').promises.rename` reaches the real module's rename; the retry tests and the kill test both rely on it (`workspaceFolder.test.ts`)
- A Lima VM rehearsal of the compose profile, from boot to the checks, took about ten minutes with the setup and check scripts written while the VM booted (`sg docker -c "sh /tmp/x.sh"`, the tree by `git archive HEAD`); six stopped VMs under `~/.lima` now (`armature-token` added)
- The Mac's remote for the laptop (`dist/server.mjs` on `100.85.125.14:3100`, revision 2026-11-03) needs no rebuild for the rename retry: the change is the workspace hub's side and the revision is the same

### Decisions taken

- **Item 33, option 1** (Amos): the bounded rename retry, `RENAME_WAITS` 10 ms doubling to 1.28 s, then 503 `store_unwritable`; documenting only, and rewriting the push table less often, declined (ADR-0066 Verification, the note of 2026-11-03 night; the record's note; PR #103)
- **Step 7's four choices** (Amos, on the session's recommendations; ADR-0065 decision 7, the note of 2026-11-03; PR #104): (1) the host draws the token and prints it once when `ARMATURE_LOCAL_TOKEN` is unset, never an open hub; (2) push, bring in and deliver served as routes only under `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_ACTIONS=routes`, refused `program_action` otherwise, the setting retired with the program; (3) the compose profile keeps its push entry by that setting, the token from `docker/.env`; (4) creating a workspace is a write and needs the token
- **Names chosen without a separate decision, open to overrule:** the kind `unwritable` and 503 `store_unwritable` with `attempts`; `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_ACTIONS` with the value `routes`; the `reason` field (`local_token`, `program_action`); discovery's `localToken: required` and `actions: routes | program`

## 2026-11-03 (the Windows laptop's first checkout and LF line endings; ADR-0066's rehearsal on a real machine, item 32's two cases and the Mac's runbook over the tailnet; 503 `store_unreadable`; PRs #100 and #101)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the workspace hub plan's step 6 closed, ADR-0066's Windows rehearsal run on a real machine (2026-11-03; PRs #100 and #101 merged, the record `docs/windows-rehearsal-2026-11-03.md` on `main`, CI green at `931d44c`).** The first checkout on a Windows laptop (Node 26, Podman behind the Docker pipe, `.gitattributes` keeping every text file LF). Item 32's two cases held: a process killed between the temporary write and the rename, at both seams, on NTFS and inside OneDrive, leaves the old file whole with a complete temporary beside it, and the store reopens at the old head; the Mac's runbook, run over the tailnet against the Mac's instance, terminated the hub in thirty rounds with nothing lost and pushed 542 commits. A folder inside OneDrive works while the sync client runs (hydration about 150 ms a file); offline, a cloud-only commit file failed opaquely with a bare 500 until a restart, and is now 503 `store_unreadable` naming the file with the open retried on the next request; the lock sweeps a killed write's temporary files; the kill is a kept test (revision 2026-11-03). Found and open: inside OneDrive the push's rewrite of `push.json` after each commit was refused twice by a Windows sharing violation on the rename (`EPERM`), reproduced once in 280 renames with the client kept busy and cleared by a second attempt 15 ms later; push recovery resumed each time. 465 tests. **Next: Amos's decision on a bounded rename retry (item 33; the record's §6), then step 7's first items (item 17); or item 30 under Podman here.**

### Done

- **The machine.** The repository's first checkout on Windows: Node 26.13 and npm 12, Podman 6.1 in a WSL2 machine answering the Docker pipe (Compose v5.6 validates the project file against it), `gh` logged in, the git identity set. The CRLF checkout diagnosed: Git for Windows's system `core.autocrlf=true` and a copy in the clone's `.git/config`, which outranked the global `false` Amos set; the generated types then failed `check:types` on a tree where CI was green.

- **Item 32's two cases, the session's harness** (a child bundled with esbuild over the real `folderFiles` and file store, `fs.promises.rename` hooked to `SIGKILL` the process when the rename of a chosen file is about to happen): at the commit-file seam and the `head.json` seam, on NTFS and inside OneDrive, the old file whole, a complete `<name>.<pid>.tmp` beside it, the store reopened at the old head and wrote again. Inside OneDrive: `npm run test:files` with the temporary folder there (30 tests, 4.2 s against 2.6 s); a commit file made cloud-only by `attrib +U -P` read and rehydrated while the client ran; with the client stopped, the plain read `UNKNOWN` errno -4094, the real hub a bare 500 on every request, still 500 after the client returned (the first open kept), a fresh hub 200.

(2) The findings, the choices and the decision as a dated note under ADR-0066's Verification; a note correcting its Consequences' claim that the plan says what a hub does inside a synced folder; the plan's §10 note; deployment's caution to keep the folder outside OneDrive, iCloud Drive or Dropbox; the index row, the development plan and CLAUDE.md.

Kill mode, thirty rounds, no findings: 5,410 documents acknowledged, none missing after any restart, restarts 218 to 261 ms, 7 temporaries and 5 unreferenced commit files at the end, the push of 542 commits in 148 s, the remote holding every one plus the seed's 12. ### Session notes

- The session opened on a fresh Windows checkout with nothing installed; by the recheck Amos had asked for, Node, Podman and Tailscale were there. The items of the first report (identity, line endings, the npm 12 `allowScripts` block) were each explained on request and settled by Amos's own commands or a small PR, the branch created before the first edit, the commit sequence proposed first, every PR merged on "confirm and merge" and CI on `main` confirmed by job
- The kill harness went through three slips of the session's own: a regex in a heredoc that never matched, a `ReferenceError` inside the hook that by accident aborted exactly between the temporary write and the rename, and a backslash the heredoc dropped; `String.fromCharCode(92)` and, in the kept test, a check that needs no path normalization ended it. esbuild's CommonJS output reads `rename` off the module object at each call, so replacing it on `fs.promises` reaches the real module
- The offline case was simulated by stopping OneDrive's process and starting it again from its install path, after a cloud-only file had been made; the hub was run from its bundle with stdout and stderr to files, which is how the bare 500's cause was seen
- Amos chose options (b) and (d) on the session's recommendation and the kept test once esbuild turned out to be `app`'s own devDependency; the controlled reproduction of the afternoon's refusal failed twice (pace, size) before keeping the sync client busy reproduced it, which is what sized the retry
- The Mac's runbook was read whole before anything ran; its bundle's hash was compared with the local build; probing the remote with `Armature-Version: 2026-11-03` showed writes refused (`unsupported_version`) and reads served, so the runbook ran the older bundle it came with. Auto mode refused a forced `git reset --hard` and a `sed` over the memory file; each was done another way

### Facts from 2026-11-03 worth keeping

- **A remote serves reads and refuses writes from a client at a newer revision** (`unsupported_version`, 400, with `supported`), so a hub bundle built from `main` ahead of an instance cannot create a branch there or push until the instance is updated (ADR-0059; seen against the Mac's instance)
- **OneDrive skips `.tmp` files:** a temporary of `writeWhole` never gets the cloud filter's reparse point and is not uploaded; only the file renamed over is the sync client's concern (the record, §4.1)
- **A cloud-only file under a stopped sync client reads as libuv's `UNKNOWN`, errno -4094, syscall `read`;** the open succeeds and the read fails. Hydration while the client runs costs about 150 ms a file (`docs/windows-rehearsal-2026-11-03.md` §4)
- **A rename over a file the sync client holds is refused with `EPERM` (errno -4048)**, intermittently, while the client is busy; one more attempt after tens of milliseconds succeeded (§5). graceful-fs retries `EPERM`, `EACCES` and `EBUSY` on `rename` for up to 60 s on Windows
- **Git for Windows sets `core.autocrlf=true` in its system config, and a clone may carry it in `.git/config` too,** which outranks a global setting; `.gitattributes` now governs (PR #100). `git ls-files --eol` shows index against working tree
- **The Mac's hub reached over Tailscale:** `ARMATURE_HUB_BIND` on the Tailscale address and `ARMATURE_ALLOWED_HOSTS` naming it and the tailnet name; the laptop's workspace hub as a plain Node process needs none of this machine's missing files, since the seed lives on the Mac (the record, §1; the laptop's facts are in memory)
- **`npm run build:hub` is `app`'s script, not the root's;** esbuild 0.28 is `app`'s devDependency, so a test may build a child with it

### Decisions taken

- **Options (b) and (d) built; (a) and (c) to step 7** (Amos, 2026-11-03, on the session's recommendation): a clear 503 for a cloud-only file and the open retried; stray temporaries swept with the lock; a warning or refusal on a synced folder and pinning left to the desktop program that chooses the folder (ADR-0066 Verification, the note of 2026-11-03; the plan's §10 note)
- **The kill kept as a test, a child built with esbuild at test time** (the same note; `workspaceFolder.test.ts`)
- **Every text file LF by `.gitattributes`** (PR #100)
- **The rehearsal record committed straight to `main`** (Amos's instruction, a one-off; the branch rule stands)
- **Names chosen without a separate decision, open to overrule:** `store_unreadable`, the kind `unreadable`, `EIO` treated like `UNKNOWN`, `Retry-After: 5`, `sweepTemporaries` inside `lockFolder`
- **Open:** a bounded rename retry in `writeWhole` (the record's §6; SESSION.md item 33)

## 2026-11-02, night (step 6 closed: the folder the default, then the one local store by ADR-0066 decision 4; the file store's suites on a Windows runner; PRs #97 to #99)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the workspace hub plan's step 6 closed but for two real-machine cases, the folder the one local store (2026-11-02, night; PRs #97 to #99 merged, CI green on `main` at `d6050cc`).** The role names the store: a workspace hub runs over the file-backed store in `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_DIR`, opened and locked by its host, and a remote hub over TerminusDB; `ARMATURE_STORE`, the setting of one day, is retired and refused if set (ADR-0066 decision 4, taken by Amos that night: no evidence that offline history had mattered to developers, both stores holding the same history in any case, against doubled suites and rehearsals). The `workspace` compose service runs the hub's image over the folder in the volume `workspace-folder`, reads no `TERMINUS_*` variable and stays off the store network; the workspace and cycle suites run over the file store alone, the two-store contract test holding the folder to the remote's store; both compose changes were rehearsed on fresh VMs. CI's sixth job, `files`, runs the no-store suites on `windows-latest` (`npm run test:files`, `app/vitest.files.config.mts`), where the first run found Windows refusing to flush a directory handle, so the folder flush after a rename is skipped on `win32`; the file flush, the rename over an existing file, 300 replacements of one file, a store over 40 commits reopened from disk and the lock all pass on NTFS with Defender on. 461 tests. **Next: step 7's first items (the per-launch secret, push as the program's own action, `api.<site>` and the `oidc` amendments of item 3), or, when a Windows machine allows, the two real-machine cases (item 32).**

### Facts from 2026-11-02 (night) worth keeping

- **Windows refuses to flush a directory handle** (`fsync` on a folder opened read-only: `EPERM`, every write on the CI runner); NTFS journals a rename itself, so `syncFolder` skips the flush on `win32` (`app/lib/workspaceFolder.ts`; ADR-0066 Verification). The file flush, the rename over an existing file and the exclusive-create lock work as on macOS and Linux
- **A stray `.tmp` file is ignored when a folder opens:** only `.json` files under `commits/` are read and only branch-named folders at the root (`filestore/store.ts` `load`; tested in `workspaceFolder.test.ts`)
- **The no-store suites run anywhere with `npm run test:files`** (`app/vitest.files.config.mts`: the folder test and the file store's two module tests; no global setup); CI's `files` job runs them on Windows
- **A folder mounted into the hub's image must be writable by its `node` user** (`USER node` in `hub.Dockerfile`), or the hub exits before it prints; a named volume is initialized with the right owner
- **Every `ArmatureDocument` needs `label`**; the write route's 400 names the path
- **Both Lima VMs of this night are stopped, not deleted** (`armature-step6`, `armature-d4`); five stopped VMs now sit under `~/.lima`
