## 2026-11-02, evening (the workspace hub's step 6 in part: the file-backed store, its ids and patch, the two-store contract test, both suites over both stores, the measurement; PRs #95 and #96)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the workspace hub plan's step 6 built in part: the file-backed store (2026-11-02, evening; PRs #95 and #96 merged, CI green on `main` at `8acaa14`).** The second implementation of the store interfaces is a folder of files (`app/lib/api/filestore/`): one folder per branch holding `head.json` and immutable `commits/<id>.json` files named by the SHA-256 of their content, the state at a commit replayed from the parent chain into one working map and kept in memory; a workspace's branch folder is its workspace folder, `main/` the mirror and `at.<commit>/` the snapshots, so the clone, the snapshots and the bring-in run unchanged over either store. `ids.ts` mints Hash ids by check AI's formula from the store's own schema entries (every id of the seed reproduced, the pipeline on both stores returning the same ids), `patch.ts` emits and applies TerminusDB's patch operations, the host (`app/lib/workspaceFolder.ts`) serves the folder and holds a `lock`. Opt-in by `ARMATURE_STORE=files` on a workspace hub; `docker/` is untouched. The two-store contract test asks every read route of both hubs with the same answers; `workspace.test.ts` and `cycle.test.ts` run over both local stores; opening 20,000 documents over 2,000 commits from disk takes half a second (ADR-0066 Verification). 493 tests. **Next: close step 6: the default flipped to `files` on a workspace hub with the compose profile's `ARMATURE_STORE=terminusdb` and a fresh-VM rehearsal (rule 5), the Windows rehearsal when a machine allows, then ADR-0066 decision 4 (Amos); or step 7's first items.**

### Session notes

- The session opened on SESSION.md's "First" and Amos said "start"; the branch was created before the first edit this time. The first pull request was built as proposed (the minter, the store, the seam, the contract test), the diff included rather than deferred since the patch module came to about a hundred lines with its applier.
- The contract test found two things worth knowing: a walk's edge order follows the store's listing order (TerminusDB lists by class then id, the file store by id), so the comparison sorts edges and nodes but keeps an item's parts in order; and the pipeline is the strongest id check: a junction and an attestation written through both hubs came back with identical Hash ids, so the formula holds under the pipeline's own filling of `attestedBy` and `asOf`.
- The measurement exposed the replay's cost before the suites did: remembering a copied map at every commit of a chain made opening scale with commits × documents; one working map fixed it and the measurement was redone.
- A read-only probe of the local store was refused by auto mode once because it carried a POST to the seed's `main`; the rerun without the write was fine. zsh does not word-split an unquoted variable, so `curl $AUTH` with `AUTH="-u user:pass"` failed authentication until quoted inline. Vitest prints a passing test's console output nowhere here; a probe writes to a file instead.
- The seam held without touching a workspace module: `records.ts`'s pattern (an interface in the API, the folder on the host, memory in the tests) was reused for `StoreFiles`, and `createStore`'s dispatch on the config union meant the three operator-side call sites (`clone.ts`, `snapshots.ts`, `bringIn.ts`) run over the file store as they are.

### Facts from 2026-11-02 (evening) worth keeping

- **Opening a workspace from disk** (ADR-0066 Verification): 5,000 documents over 1,000 commits in about 180 ms from 2.8 MB of commit files; 20,000 over 2,000 in about 500 ms from 10 MB; a document's history in milliseconds; heap growth under 50 MB, indicative only.
- **A nested id has the form** `<parent>/<field>/<position>/<Class>/<16 URL-safe base64 characters>`, the position only inside a List (an `externalRefs` Set member carries none); a top-level Random id is `<Class>/<16 characters>` (`ids.ts`).
- **A type query lists subclasses** (`type=DesignRecord` returns every record); the file store does the same through `SchemaIndex.descendants`. Within a type both stores list by id; across types TerminusDB lists by class first.
- **TerminusDB returns a document's keys in order:** `@id`, `@type`, then alphabetical; the file store stores them so.
- **The `workspace` compose profile still runs TerminusDB** (`ARMATURE_STORE` unset); flipping the default is a compose change and therefore a rehearsal (rule 5).

### Decisions taken

- **The layout as built is branch folders** (`main/`, `at.<commit>/`, a workspace's folder with its records beside `commits/`), the clone's commit file standing for `base.jsonld`, `head.json` beside the commits, `store.json` with a format number, no `origin` in a commit file (it is `push.json`'s `broughtIn`): ADR-0066 decision 2, note of 2026-11-02 evening; plan §10. Open to overrule.
- **The file store is opt-in** (`ARMATURE_STORE=files`, a workspace hub only) until step 6 closes, so `docker/` is untouched: ADR-0066 Status; plan §14 step 6.
- **The patch's list segmentation may differ from TerminusDB's, its operations do not:** ADR-0066 decision 5, note; `docs/api.md` (history and diff).
- **Ids are minted from the store's own schema entries,** not the generated types, so a folder holding another schema version mints by that schema: ADR-0066 decision 6, note.
- **Both workspace suites run over both local stores on every run** rather than a CI-only second pass: plan §13.

## 2026-11-02 (check AI and ADR-0066's acceptance; the workspace hub's step 5: snapshots, the kept log, forwarding, remote commit identifiers; PRs #93 and #94)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; check AI run, ADR-0066 accepted, the workspace hub plan's step 5 built (2026-11-02; PRs #93 and #94 merged, CI green on `main` at `a3d687d`).** TerminusDB's Hash identifiers are computable from the key fields (check AI: each value a full IRI for a reference or an enum value, a string or an integer as itself, an absent `Optional` as the literal `+none+`, joined with `+`, percent-encoded except `!$&'()*,;=@` and the unreserved characters, SHA-256), so ADR-0066 decision 6's first branch holds and the ADR is accepted with its notes on ADR-0055 and ADR-0065. On a workspace hub a remote commit identifier now works wherever a commit works: `workspace/store.ts` wraps the adapter and resolves one through the records (the base, pushed and brought-in commits, snapshots) and then the remote when reachable, else 404 `unknown_ref` with `remote_needed`; a clone takes a full export at each older `asOf` commit its records name, most-referenced first within `ARMATURE_SNAPSHOTS` (20), each a branch `at.<commit>` recorded in `snapshots.json`, and keeps the branch's whole log in `log.json`, with which `GET /branches/:name/log` continues past the clone; bring in extends both; `If-Match` may name a remote identifier; revision 2026-11-02. Not built: subject-only snapshots beyond the budget (forwarding covers them until a bank exceeds it), the local merge preview, the migration window's close, the `oidc` parts. **Next: step 6, the file-backed store (plan §14; ADR-0066 decisions 2 and 6), starting with the commit files and the in-memory index behind the extracted interface, check AI's formula minting the ids (What's Next item 17).**

Reloading the schema and seed (item 20's remedy) restored them. The branch for step 5 was created only at commit time; nothing had been committed on `main`

### Facts from 2026-11-02 worth keeping

- **TerminusDB's Hash id** is `<Class>/` + SHA-256 (lower-case hex) of the key values as the Lexical key would print them: a reference as its full IRI under `@base`, an enum value under `@schema` (`GrantRole/Owner`), a string or integer as itself, an absent `Optional` or an empty Set as `+none+`, joined with `+`, percent-encoded (UTF-8, upper-case hex) except `A-Za-z0-9` and `!$&'()*,-.;=@_~` (check AI; ADR-0066 decision 6, note; development plan §9)
- **An attestation, finding or metric never exists at its own `asOf`**, which names the commit before it was written; a test of a read at `asOf` reads the subject
- **The local store slows past about 700 commits on `main` with the grants that come with them:** `app.test.ts`'s branch deletion and dry-run merge and `race.test.ts`'s `branch_busy` case cross the 15-second timeout; `load_schema.js --recreate` then `seed_data.js` restores it (item 20; the tooling memory)
- **Vitest prints `console.log` from a test only when the test fails** (`silent: 'passed-only'` behavior); make a probe fail at its end, or write to a file (the tooling memory)
- **The snapshot budget's measurement:** the seed's `main` names two older commits, each snapshot 63 and 68 documents, about 90 KB apiece; the default is 20 commits per workspace (ADR-0066, Verification)

## 2026-11-01, night (the workspace cycle: push, the merge checked before it commits, bring in and deliver; the branch log, `document_moved`, ADR-0067 decision 10; PRs #91 and #92)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the workspace cycle built under `header` (plan §14 steps 1 to 4; PRs #91 and #92 merged 2026-11-01 night, CI green on `main` at `28ab878`).** A workspace hub creates and clones a workspace, pushes its local commits as one batch write each through the remote's `/api/v1` with `If-Match` (`workspace/push.ts`: `push.json` beside `workspace.json`, every `asOf` rewritten from the commit table, the trailers `Armature-Workspace-Commit`, `Armature-Authored-At` and `Armature-Workspace-Part`, an agent's commit as the agent acting for the owner, the split rule in `split.ts` at the hub's own cap, an in-flight marker settled from the remote's log), brings in the remote's merge of `main` as one local commit and delivers into `main` after a dry run confirms `main` is in (`workspace/bringIn.ts`); the remote checks every merge before it commits on a transient branch, 422 with the target unchanged, with `dryRun` and `If-Match`; a workspace is written and pushed by its owner alone, with a warning at creation when the remote would refuse the push (ADR-0068 decision 9); an agent as the credential itself reads and never writes on a remote hub (ADR-0067 decision 10); a write without `If-Match` that loses its race over the same document is 409 `document_moved`, never an overwrite; `GET /branches/:name/log` is the branch log. The three actions are routes under `/workspace/:name/` until the desktop program exists. Not built: a read on a workspace hub at a pushed commit by its remote identifier (with step 5), the local merge preview, the migration window's close, the `oidc` parts. **Next: step 5, the clone's snapshots and forwarding (plan §8, §16 question 3), or check AI toward ADR-0066's acceptance (item 26), Amos's call.**

### Session notes

- The session opened on SESSION.md's "First", took two decisions on options with a recommendation (a replace need not carry `If-Match`, the hub refuses a lost race over the same document; push driven by a route until the program exists), then built steps 3 and 4 in order, each PR proposed as a commit sequence, committed on Amos's word, merged on green on "confirm and merge"
- Check AL settled the split rule's limit at the hub's own cap and showed the store resolves a `@ref` before its `@capture`, so a group is kept whole for the boundary's sake, not the order's; check AK made the merge route take the ADR's fallback, the head compared before and after, the residual window one request wide
- Push had three details the plan did not spell out: the local pipeline carries a `User` copy onto a branch, which must not be replayed (the remote carries its own); the local hub writes `Armature-On-Behalf-Of` on an agent's commit, which the remote refuses from a client, so it is stripped before replay and written again there; the store's nested ids on fragments are left out, a part's identity being its `fragmentId`
- The whole-branch merge check exposed a flaw in both scratch copies of the seed: their `asOf` values named commits of the original database. Loading the copy in two commits, undated records then dated ones with `asOf` naming the first, is the shape the seed itself has
- The version test failed five cases once when run beside lint, and passed alone; the CI watcher for `main` first caught a build-and-deploy workflow, not the CI workflow, so a run is now checked by workflow name

### Facts from 2026-11-01 (night) worth keeping

- **A scratch copy of the seed must be loaded in two commits** so every `asOf` names a commit the copy holds: the operator's `check` and the merge route's check both run constraint 13 over the whole branch (`authorization.test.ts`, `cycle.test.ts`)
- **`/api/apply` ignores `TerminusDB-Data-Version`** (check AK): a merge cannot be conditioned on the target head; the hub compares heads around the apply (`routes/branches.ts`; ADR-0025 decision 5, note; development plan §9)
- **The store resolves a `@ref` before its `@capture` in one list, and inside a Hash key's fields** (check AL2); the split rule's order is for the boundary between parts (`split.ts`)
- **The local pipeline carries a `User` copy onto a branch in the same commit as a first write by that user**, so a local commit's documents may include a `User`, which a push leaves out (`push.ts` `documentsOf`)
- **A hub-written trailer on a local commit is refused by the remote** (`reserved_trailer`), so a push strips `Armature-On-Behalf-Of` and `Armature-Relayed-By` before replay (`trailers.ts` `withoutTrailers`)
- **`gh run list --branch main` returns the newest run of any workflow**; pass `--workflow ci.yml` to watch the contract suite
- **Running `version.test.ts` beside `npm run lint` produced five spurious failures once**; the file passes alone and in the full suite

### Decisions taken

- **A replace need not carry `If-Match`; the hub refuses a lost race over the same document with 409 `document_moved`** (Amos, on the session's recommendation over the review's B2 advice; ADR-0025 decision 7, note of 2026-11-01; `race.test.ts`)
- **Push, bring in and deliver are routes under `/workspace/:name/` until the desktop program exists**, withdrawn or gated at step 7; whether the compose `workspace` profile keeps an entry is decided then (Amos; ADR-0065 decision 7, note of 2026-11-01)
- **The merge check runs on a transient branch, the target head compared before and after** (ADR-0025 decision 5, note of 2026-11-01, on check AK)
- **Bring-in is the second bounded route-side use of the operator methods** (ADR-0062, note of 2026-11-01; `store.test.ts` names both)
- **A workspace is written and pushed by its owner, or an agent acting for them, anyone else 403 naming the owner** (plan §7; ADR-0065 decision 7, note)
- **Names chosen without a separate decision, open to overrule:** `document_moved`, `push_in_progress`, `group_too_large`, `unpushed_commits`, `bring_in_first`; `forbidden`'s `credential` and `owner`; `permission` values `create`, `push`, `bring-in`, `deliver`; the transient prefix `operator-merge-`; `parts: 0` with `broughtIn: true` for a brought-in commit; `armature/branches`'s `logCount` and `logPage`
- **Deferred with a trigger:** a read at a pushed commit by its remote identifier (step 5); the local merge preview (a cost); the engine scoped to a merge's changed documents (a large branch); the compose profile's push entry (step 7)

## 2026-11-01, evening (ADR-0068 and ADR-0067 decisions 3, 6 and 8: grants on `main`, branch ownership, the permission check, agents acting for people; PR #90)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; ADR-0068 built under `header`, with ADR-0067 decisions 3, 6 and 8 (2026-11-01 evening; PR #90 merged, CI green on `main` at `2b39ed3`).** Authority is `Grant` documents on `main` (`app/lib/api/grants.ts`; constraints 21 and 22): whoever creates a branch owns it, by a grant the hub writes before the branch exists and revokes before it is deleted; a remote hub checks every write, merge, delete, grant and registration against the grants in force (`authorization.ts`, decision 4's table) and refuses with 403 `forbidden`; `GET /users/me` carries the caller's grants and `GET /discover` lists `armature/authorization` with `ownerlessBranches: "writable"`, since the migration window's close is not built; the operator's `check` reports a grant naming no branch and a branch with no owner; the seed makes the designer `Administrator` and `Maintainer` of every branch. `Armature-Agent` names an agent acting for the person in `Armature-User`: the agent is the author, the person's authority applies, the hub writes `Armature-On-Behalf-Of` and refuses it from clients (`trailers.ts`). Not built: decision 4's `oidc` rows, decision 9's warning at workspace creation, ADR-0067 decision 10's refusal. **Next: push, plan §14 step 3, platform check AL first, with item 19's branch log beside it (What's Next item 17).**

### Done

- **PR #90, eight commits, one logical change each.** (1) The schema: `Grant` (category `infrastructure`, Hash key over `user`, `role`, `branch`; `GrantRole` `Owner`, `Maintainer`, `Administrator`, `Relay`; `GrantStatus` `Active`, `Revoked`; `rationale`, `relayedUsers`, `expires`), `User.issuer`, and `User`'s documentation corrected (item 25), with the generated types, the Zod schemas and the appendix. (2) `Grant` writable through the document routes, on `main` only (constraint 21, 400 `grants_live_on_main`, judged by the branch a write is for, `WriteOptions.target`, so the operator's `put` on `main` passes); the holder's kind suits the role (constraint 22, `grant_holder_kind`, `relayed_users_not_relay`; `invariants/grant.ts`); `Grant` in the `full` slice; `authorization.test.ts` over a scratch database loaded from the seed's export. (3) `grants.ts`: the `Owner` grant written through the pipeline before `POST /branches` creates the branch, which therefore needs an identity, and every grant naming a branch revoked before `DELETE`; `check`'s two rules `grant_without_branch` and `branch_without_owner` when `main` is checked through a store that lists branches; `deleteScratchBranch(name, { as })` revoking first as a marked commit, used by every suite's teardown and the global setup, scratch names widened to `<handle>_<scratch>`; the Design Graph draws design records only; the pseudonymous export rewrites a handle prefix in `Grant.branch`, `handleOf` moved to `identity.ts`. (4) `authorization.ts`: decision 4's table as `refusal()`, grants read once per request (`c.set('grants')`), `requirePermission` on merge, delete and registration, `requireWrite` on both document writes (a grant judged as a grant, anything else as a write), 403 `forbidden` with `permission` and `branch`, `role` or `agent`; ownerless branches writable; nothing on a workspace hub; `GET /users/me` with `grants`; the capability `armature/authorization` (`ownerlessBranches`); `Grant` listed by `user`, the guard scoped by `inheritsFrom(type, 'DesignRecord')`; the seed's two grants; the client's `me()` type. (5) `trailers.ts` and `Armature-Agent` in `resolveUser`: the person first, the agent second, `ResolvedUser.onBehalfOf`, `principalOf`, `commitMessage` appending `Armature-On-Behalf-Of` (into an existing trailer block), `refuseReservedTrailers` in `readEnvelope`, `unknown_agent`, `not_an_agent`, `not_a_person`; the preflight allows the header; the client's `agent` option. (6) Dated notes on ADR-0067 and ADR-0068 (what is built, what waits), ADR-0015, 0021, 0025, 0031, 0032, 0062 and 0065; the index; plan §14. (7) The reference (identity rules, a Grants section, rules 21 and 22, the codes), the schema guide, the change log (riding 2026-11-01), the explainer (§15.5), README, deployment, the research path, demo coverage (an Authorization row; 26 capabilities), the client README, CLAUDE.md within budget, PROJECT_CONTEXT. (8) The smoke test's record count, 69 with the grants
- Tests 357 to 389; the suite leaves `main` clean for `check`, since every teardown revokes its branch's grants

### Session notes

- The rhythm: each commit proposed with its files and choices, built, gated by the whole suite, then committed on Amos's word; three fresh seeds along the way, because a reseed in place doubles the seed's history and the suite notices
- Building constraint 21 as "the branch written is `main`" would have refused the operator's `put` on `main`, which writes through a transient branch; the pipeline learned the branch a write is for (`WriteOptions.target`), and the seed's and the operator's first-administrator paths both have tests
- Grants put branch names into documents for the first time, and a workspace branch is named after its owner; the export test's blunt "no `demo-designer` anywhere" caught the leak twice, in `Grant.branch` and then in the hub's own revocation rationale. The field is rewritten; the rationale names nothing
- The app test registers a reviewer and deleted that `User` in its teardown; with grants referencing them the store refused, and a "Test U" user leaked into the export test. A person who ever held a grant is permanent, which is the ADR's intent; the fixture deletes its grants first through the operator
- CLAUDE.md went 1.3 KB over budget with constraints 21 and 22 and the identity facts; four passages moved to the homes that already held them (the terms to `deployment.md`'s table, the ADR summaries to the index, the generator's mappings to its header, the junction pattern to the schema guide) and the `Grant` row to the schema and the reference; it ends at 31.8 KB. The viewer's smoke test failed once in CI on the header's record count, 67 to 69, the only CI failure of the day

### Facts from 2026-11-01 (evening) worth keeping

- **A `User` that has ever held a grant cannot be deleted**: the store keeps references whole, and a revoked grant still references its holder (`app.test.ts`, the users teardown; ADR-0068's intent)
- **`seed_data.js` without `--recreate` keeps the earlier seed's commits**, so a document's history doubles and `intelligence.test.ts` fails on it; reset with `node scripts/load_schema.js --recreate` then the seed (item 20's pattern)
- **The operator's `put` writes on a transient `operator-…` branch and applies onto its target**, so a rule about *which branch* a write is on must read `WriteOptions.target` (`write.ts`; constraint 21)
- **Vitest runs the files one at a time, but a stale local store carries state between runs**: every teardown must undo what its tests wrote on `main`, since nothing else does (`deleteScratchBranch`, the grants deletion)
- **The Design Graph's header counts every document at the ref**, people and grants included (`GraphView.tsx` `recordCount`; `app/e2e/graph.spec.ts`)

### Decisions taken

- **ADR-0068 built whole under `header`, before push, with ADR-0067 decisions 3, 6 and 8** (Amos's "continue" at each step; the ADRs' Status notes of 2026-11-01; plan §14)
- **Constraint 21 judges by the branch a write is for, so the operator's `put` on `main` writes the first administrator** (`write.ts`; ADR-0062 note)
- **`expires` is valid on every role; `relayedUsers` on `Relay` alone** (chosen while building, open to overrule; ADR-0068 Status note)
- **An `Administrator` holding nothing else does not write, merge or delete; they grant themselves first** (the table read literally; ADR-0068 Status note; `authorization.test.ts`)
- **The migration window stays open and discovery says so** (`ownerlessBranches: "writable"`), its closing undecided (ADR-0068 Status note; What's Next item 31)
- **The pseudonymous and institutional profiles rewrite a handle prefix in `Grant.branch`; the hub's own rationales name neither a branch nor a person** (ADR-0031 note)
- **`deleteScratchBranch` revokes the branch's grants first, as a marked commit, and takes `--as`** (ADR-0062 note; `deployment.md`)
- **The change-log entry rides revision 2026-11-01** (CLAUDE.md's rule; `client-changes.md`)
- **Names chosen without a separate decision, open to overrule:** `grants_live_on_main`, `grant_holder_kind`, `relayed_users_not_relay`; `grant_without_branch`, `branch_without_owner`; `forbidden`'s `permission` values `write`, `merge`, `delete`, `grant`, `register` with `branch`, `role` or `agent`; `ownerlessBranches`; `unknown_agent`, `not_an_agent`, `not_a_person`, `reserved_trailer`; `WriteOptions.target`; `principalOf`, `onBehalfOf`

## 2026-11-01 (step 2, workspace mode: the role, create and clone, the compose profile; PRs #86 to #88)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the workspace hub plan's step 2 built (2026-11-01; PRs #86 to #88 merged, CI green on `main` at `0d00e73`).** A hub's configuration travels on each request (`createApp`, `HubConfig` in `app/lib/api/config.ts`), so a remote and a workspace hub run in one process. `ARMATURE_ROLE=workspace` makes a workspace hub: it refuses branch creation, merge, deletion, registration and writes to `main` with 400 `remote_only` and says so in `GET /discover` (revision 2026-11-01); it creates a workspace on its remote and clones it (`POST /workspace`, the one route-side use of the operator methods, ADR-0062 note), mirrors `main`'s schema and users, and reports status, its records in `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_DIR`. The compose file has two networks, a guarded `public` profile and a `workspace` profile, rehearsed on a fresh VM; a hub under `header` listens beyond loopback only with `ARMATURE_TRUST_NETWORK=1` (ADR-0067 decision 8, in part). **Next: before step 3 (push), ADR-0068 whole and ADR-0067 decisions 3, 6 and the rest of 8 (What's Next item 17).**

### Done

- **PR #86, the role and the refusals:** `config.ts` defines `HubConfig` (store, identity, allow-lists, export key, role, remote), read from the environment per request by default; `createApp(source)` sets it first, routes make stores with `storeFor(c, ref)`, `startHub({ config })` takes one; source scans hold that only `config.ts` reads the environment and makes a store. `ARMATURE_ROLE` and `ARMATURE_REMOTE_URL`, checked when read and once at startup. `role.ts`'s `REMOTE_ONLY` table registers the guards ahead of the routes and fills `/discover`'s `remoteOnly` (on `armature/branches`, `armature/users`) and `readOnlyBranches` (on `armature/documents`) beside a top-level `role`; a workspace hub registers no one. Revision 2026-11-01, `CLIENT_REVISION` with it
- **PR #87, create and clone:** platform check AN; `ensureDatabase` and `deleteDatabase` on `OperatorStore`. `POST /workspace` (`{ name, from? }` creates `<handle>_<name>` on the remote, `{ existing }` clones), `GET /workspace`, `GET /workspace/:name/status` (base, local commits since the clone, remote head with `moved`, or `{ reachable: false }`); the remote called through `@armature/client`, its refusals passed through with `remote: true`, 502 `remote_unreachable`; `workspace/clone.ts` mirrors `main` (nothing written when unchanged), loads the export, replaces an interrupted clone and refuses a branch with work (409); records through a `WorkspaceRecords` interface, the folder in `lib/workspaceFolder.ts` (flush and rename, paths guarded by `BRANCH_NAME`, now in `http.ts`). `armature/workspace` listed and accepted only by a workspace hub, `workspace_only` on a remote. The client's `workspace.*`; a second 2026-11-01 entry in `client-changes.md`; notes on ADR-0062, ADR-0031 and ADR-0065 decision 2
- **PR #88, the compose profile:** `bindRefusal` in `lib/hub.ts` (ADR-0067 decision 8's bound on `header`); the store network `armature-store` (TerminusDB, the hubs, the operator) beside the front, still `default` named `armature`; `public-guard`, a one-shot service Caddy waits on, refusing an unset or `admin` password; the `workspace` service on loopback 3101; `ARMATURE_TRUST_NETWORK=1` on each hub service, not in the image; `/workspaces` owned by `node` in the Dockerfile; CI checks the bare image refuses. `deployment.md`'s new section with the rehearsal; item 24's two-roles rewrite (README, explainer §10.1 and glossary, schema guide, `api.md`); ADR-0067 decision 8's note
- Tests 326 to 357; bundle smoke tests of the role, of a bundled remote and workspace hub, and the VM rehearsal

### Session notes

- Reading `app.ts` for PR 1 showed the plan's one-process cycle test could not run while every setting was an ambient `process.env` read; that became the session's first question (per-app configuration, as recommended) and a no-behavior-change commit ahead of the role
- PR 2 needed four answers (the clone's load, where records live, who creates the local database, the slice), all as recommended. The first cycle-test run found the clone "different" from the remote; a probe showed only key order differed, so the comparison and the mirror use `canonical`, as `export.test.ts` already did. A test meant to show `bad_name` created a real remote branch (`demo-designer_-bad` is legal) and was cleaned up by hand
- PR 3: two compose probes settled the password guard before the file was written; the rehearsal covered the guard, an in-place upgrade of `main`'s seeded public instance, the isolation, the bare image, the workspace cycle across a restart, closed ports and a power cycle. Colima and Podman Desktop were left unrehearsed by Amos's choice
- CLAUDE.md hit its budget twice: once by moving the TerminusDB re-sync sentence to the skill that already held it, once by replacing the workspace-terms bullet with a pointer to plan §2, which defines them; it ends at 32.0 KB with 9 bytes free. Each PR merged on Amos's "confirm and merge" (one typed "conform"), CI confirmed on `main`

### Facts from 2026-11-01 worth keeping

- **Compose evaluates `${VAR:?}` for every service, whatever profiles are active, and a failed dependency marked `required: false` only warns**, so a per-profile requirement is a strict dependency on a service only that profile has (ADR-0067 decision 8, note of 2026-11-01; the compose file's comment)
- **A new TerminusDB database's `main` already holds one `InitialCommit` by `system`**; GET `/api/db/admin/<db>` is 404 for an absent database; creating one twice is 400 `api:DatabaseAlreadyExists` (check AN; `docs/development-plan.md` §9)
- **A document reloaded into the store comes back with its keys sorted, where one written earlier may come back with `asOf` out of order**, so documents are compared with `canonical` (`fragments.ts`), never `JSON.stringify` (`workspace/clone.ts`, `workspace.test.ts`)
- **Ubuntu 24.04's packages give Compose 2.40; Docker Desktop here has 5.0**; both honor `depends_on` conditions and `required` (`docs/deployment.md`, the rehearsal)
- **Expired without a home:** "the store contends across branches" (concurrent writers to different branches answer `Transaction retry count exceeded`); no lettered check was written, and the suite still runs files one at a time

### Decisions taken

- **Each request carries its hub's configuration** (`createApp`, `HubConfig`), over AsyncLocalStorage in the host and over a remote hub in a child process (Amos; `config.ts`'s header, PR #86)
- **A workspace hub's clone loads through the operator-side methods, bounded to `workspace/clone.ts`**, over the write pipeline and a loader outside the API (Amos; ADR-0062 and ADR-0031, notes of 2026-11-01)
- **A workspace's record lives in `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_DIR/<branch>/workspace.json`** behind an interface, over trailers on the clone commit (Amos; ADR-0065 decision 2, note of 2026-11-01)
- **The workspace hub creates its local database when absent** (Amos; ADR-0062 note; check AN)
- **A clone takes the `full` profile until plan §16 question 4 is settled** (Amos; ADR-0031 note)
- **Step 2's rehearsal is a fresh Lima VM with Docker Engine; Colima and Podman Desktop later** (Amos; `docs/deployment.md`, plan §14 step 2; What's Next item 30)
- **Names chosen without a separate decision, open to overrule:** `ARMATURE_ROLE`, `ARMATURE_REMOTE_URL`, `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_DIR`, `ARMATURE_TRUST_NETWORK` (ADR-0067's), `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_DB`, `ARMATURE_WORKSPACE_PORT`; `remoteOnly`, `readOnlyBranches`; `remote_only` reused for writes to `main`; `workspace_only`, `workspace_exists`, `remote_unreachable`, `remote_error`; `existing`, `from`, `local`, `clonedAt`; the handle as the external identifier's local part; `profile: 'full'`; a 60-second remote deadline; `armature-store`, `public-guard`, port 3101

## 2026-10-31 (step 1, the store adapter interface; the deep review's B1, B2 and small fixes; PRs #83 to #85)

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8; the workspace hub plan's step 1 built, with the deep review's B1, B2 and small fixes (2026-10-31; PRs #83 to #85 merged, CI green on `main` at `6fcb64f`).** The store adapter is three interfaces by role (`DocumentReader`, `Store`, `OperatorStore`) over one class, `TerminusStore`, its refusals reported by a store-neutral `kind` (ADR-0055 and ADR-0066, notes of 2026-10-31). A request that reads more than once is answered at one commit, and a write checks its rules at the head it captured and commits only onto it (ADR-0025 decisions 3 and 7, notes; revision 2026-10-31). The review's small confirmed fixes are in (What's Next item 28), except the merge route's head precondition, which waits on check AK. ADR-0065, 0067 and 0068 stay accepted and unbuilt apart from ADR-0067 decision 7; ADR-0066 stays Proposed until check AI runs. **Next: plan §14 step 2, workspace mode with TerminusDB as the test bed, with item 27's compose changes under one fresh-VM rehearsal (What's Next item 17).**

### Done

- **PR #83, plan §14 step 1, the adapter interface:** `app/lib/api/store.ts` exports `DocumentReader` (documents at one ref: what the engine, the intelligence reads, `check` and `discover` use; the six methods the plan named plus `getSchema`), `Store` (adding writes, history, branches and merge) and `OperatorStore` (adding the four operator methods, reached only through `createOperatorStore`); the class is `TerminusStore`. The engine's `WriteContext`, the `Graph`, `check` and `requireCommit` are typed against `DocumentReader`; `repair.ts` takes an `OperatorStore`. `StoreError.kind` (`not_found`, `version_mismatch`, `unknown_ref`, `exists`, `id_prefix`, `id_mismatch`, `other`; later `timeout`) replaced the 15 tests of TerminusDB error names outside the adapter. Guard tests in `store.test.ts`: no module under `app/lib/api/` but the adapter names the class or reaches the operator factory. A fix the probe found: `POST /branches` from a missing origin answered 409 `branch_exists` (the route's `/exist/i` matched `api:OriginBranchDoesNotExist`); now 404 `unknown_ref`, a fix since `api.md`'s error table promised it
- **PR #84, B1 and B2:** `snapshot()` in `http.ts` resolves a branch to its head once for every intelligence read and the export; the history route reads the head before and after (equal heads prove the answer, history never being rewritten), three tries, then 503 `branch_busy` with `Retry-After: 1`; the Design Graph pages read their companion panels at the export's commit, and the Focus page re-reads its trace when it names another commit. The write pipeline captures the head, refuses a stale `If-Match` with 412 before any read, runs the lookup, the `User` copy, `asOf` and every rule at that commit and sends it as the store's data version; a write without `If-Match` that loses a race reruns whole on a fresh copy of its documents, up to three runs (`WRITE_ATTEMPTS`), then 409 `branch_moved`. Platform check AM. `app/lib/api/race.test.ts`, nine tests landing a real commit between two of a request's store calls; eight fail on the old code. Revision 2026-10-31 (`REVISIONS`, `CLIENT_REVISION`, `client-changes.md`)
- **PR #85, item 28:** `If-Match: *` refused (400 `bad_if_match`); integer, decimal and boolean query values only in exact forms (400 `bad_query`); registration retried only on `version_mismatch` or `exists`; a 10 MiB body limit (`hono/body-limit`, 413 `body_too_large`); `since` on the changes route in the branch's history, its log or a merge followed transitively through `mergeSource` and logs read at commit paths (400 `not_an_ancestor`); the 5,000-commit walk throws 500 `history_too_long`; a 30-second deadline on every store call, the dropped-read resend inside it (504 `store_timeout`); `signal` and `timeout` options on `@armature/client`; Geist from the `geist` package (1.8.1, pinned), so `next build` needs no network. A second 2026-10-31 entry in `client-changes.md`, riding the revision
- Tests 301 to 323; the review's banner records what of B1, B2, H3, H7 and the HTTP items is built

### Session notes

- Started from SESSION.md's hand-off: read `store.ts` whole and counted the importers' use of each method, which showed the error-name leak the plan had not counted; three questions (PR split, interface shape, error vocabulary), each answered as recommended
- Before mapping errors by kind, probed the store for branch creation's error names, which found the `branch_exists` bug; the fix went in its own commit so the refactor commit changed no behavior
- Before writing B1, probed whether history and log accept a commit path (history: 500; log: works), which shaped the history route and became check AM; two more questions (the write race, the history route), answered as recommended
- The race tests were run against the old pipeline by stashing the source changes; one, the export's, passed there too because its hook fired after the documents were read, and was moved before them
- Item 28 needed one decision (ancestry through merges); its six commits split three files by hunk, each commit typechecked alone with `git stash push --keep-index`, and the result compared byte for byte with the verified tree. Each PR merged on Amos's "confirm and merge", CI confirmed on `main`

### Facts from 2026-10-31 worth keeping

- **`/api/history` at a commit path is a 500; `/api/log` at one works**, listing from that commit back (platform check AM; `docs/development-plan.md` §9)
- **The store's branch-creation errors:** an existing name is `api:BranchExistsError`, a missing origin branch `api:OriginBranchDoesNotExist`, a missing origin commit a 400 with no `@type` (all HTTP 400; probed 2026-10-31; home: `TERMINUS_KINDS` in `store.ts`)
- **The importer counts SESSION.md carried** (26 modules, 15 by value) landed in PR #83 and its ADR notes; the fact is retired from the Notes
- **`geist` 1.8.1** (2026-10-16) was the newest release older than two weeks on 2026-10-31; 1.8.2 and 1.9.0 were too new

### Decisions taken

- Step 1 and B1/B2 as two pull requests, the refactor with no behavior change first (Amos, 2026-10-31; plan §14 step 1, note)
- The interface split by role, `DocumentReader` the subset a file store and Quiver's rules package need (Amos; ADR-0055 and ADR-0066, notes; plan §10)

### Facts from the 2026-10-30 evening worth carrying forward:

- **The adapter's importers, counted:** 26 modules outside the tests import `store.ts` (24 under `app/lib/api/`, `check.ts`, `repair.ts`), 15 of them taking `Store`, `createStore` or `StoreError` as values and the rest only types; `app/operator.ts` is a 27th. The invariants engine and the intelligence reads use `getDocument`, `getDocuments`, `queryDocuments`, `allDocuments`, `getCommit` and `at`; the routes and operator tools use the rest (`apply`, `createBranch`, `deleteBranch`, `createEmptyBranch`, `replaceSchema`, `insertDocuments`, `insertDocument`, `deleteDocument`, `putDocument(s)`, `log`, `head`, `listBranches`, `history`, `diff`, `getSchema`)
- **`grep` here is `ugrep`**, which refuses long `.{0,n}` regexes ("exceeds complexity limits"); `perl -ne 'while (/(...)/g) { print }'` prints the matches
- **A branch cut from an open PR's head** is rebased onto `origin/main` before its own PR (`git rebase --onto origin/main <base-commit> <branch>`), so the two PRs stand independently; the CLAUDE.md on disk then changes back to `main`'s, which is expected
- **Documentation written as "Proposed" an hour before acceptance** had to be revisited in 18 files; when an acceptance is imminent, write "ADR-00xx (Proposed; accepted ... )" placeholders sparingly, or sequence the acceptance PR first
- **`gh pr checks --watch --fail-fast`** blocks until the checks finish and exits 0 on success, which lets a merge follow in the same command once `mergeStateStatus` reads `CLEAN`

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8 with the workspace hub's decisions made and the loopback boundary built (2026-10-29 to 2026-10-30, PRs #68 to #72 and quiver#9, all merged, CI green on `main` at `aa212ac`).** Amos asked which local-development architectures had been considered (the workspace hub; a workspace library in each tool; TerminusDB's own clone and push; a full peer hub; peer sync between workspace hubs; a CRDT sync engine), why peer sync and CRDTs were declined, what full peer sync would take, and how Armature differs from Git, then chose the **file-backed store over Dolt** for the workspace hub (ADR-0066, Proposed): Dolt's strengths are branching and merging, which ADR-0065 keeps off a workspace hub, and its per-cell diff still needs TerminusDB's patch produced by hand. Going through ADR-0065's settled answers against code built since found Quiver's drafter and lint agent would be broken by decision 7's refusal of agent writes, and a DNS rebinding route to any hub beside a browser; **identity was split into ADR-0067 (authentication) and ADR-0068 (authorization)**, both Proposed: the `oidc` resolver, an agent acting for a person recorded in an `Armature-On-Behalf-Of` trailer the hub writes, relaying only under a `Relay` grant, `Grant` documents on `main` (so authority has history), branch ownership, only a maintainer merging into `main`, and the author always the caller, an agent acting for the caller, or a relayed person (Amos's question "why not any registered user" answered: one credential could then write verdicts in another person's name). Amos decided the two-client test passes, Quiver pauses as the workspace hub's first real test (its modules the starting code), ADR-0067 and ADR-0068 are prerequisites (OIDC before release; ADR-0068 and the parts of ADR-0067 that shape a push before step 3), a desktop program is the first release candidate, the clone holds as much as is reasonable, the slice is open and pseudonymous clones are deferred. ADR-0065's verification passed (check AJ and two contract tests, PR #70). **ADR-0067 decision 7, the `Host` and `Origin` checks, is built** (PR #72, `app/lib/api/boundary.ts`, contract revision 2026-10-30, rehearsed on a fresh Lima VM): building it found that Tauri's HTTP plugin sends `Origin` on every request, so desktop-shell and loopback origins are served. **Nothing technical blocks accepting ADR-0065; that and ADR-0066 to ADR-0068 are Amos's call.** *Later the same day: ADR-0065, ADR-0067 and ADR-0068 accepted; ADR-0066 stays Proposed until check AI runs.*

### Facts from 2026-10-30 worth carrying forward:

- **Tauri's HTTP plugin sends `Origin` on every request** (`tauri://localhost` on macOS and Linux, the webview's origin otherwise; `tauri-plugin-http` 2.8.3 `src/commands.rs`), unless built with `unsafe-headers`; Quiver does not. `boundary.ts` serves non-web and loopback origins for this reason
- **The store keeps commit messages byte for byte** through the `message` query parameter, CRLF and trailing newlines included (check AJ), so normalizing line endings is the hub's choice and a trailer reader accepts both
- **The API has no branch-log route**, only a document's history (`/documents/:type/:id/history`); the workspace hub's fuller clone needs one (What's Next item 19)
- **Quiver found TerminusDB's Hash ids not reproducible from the key fields** and gives junctions provisional `local-` ids renamed from the push response (`src/lib/workspace/junctions.ts` there); ADR-0066 decision 6's mapping branch is that, already built
- **`Access-Control-Expose-Headers` belongs on the response, not the preflight**; a listed origin's page reads `ETag` only that way
- **Auto mode refuses `git push --force-with-lease`**; a stale PR is brought up to date by GitHub's rebase merge itself, with CI confirmed on `main` afterward
- **The local store drifts** when Quiver's contract tests run against it; `load_schema.js --recreate` then `seed_data.js` restores the six seed commits (What's Next item 20)

## 2026-10-28

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8 with the workspace hub planned, and graph-native plugins mapped (2026-10-28, PRs #64 to #67, all merged, CI green on `main` at `077bc3b`).** Later the same day (PR #67): sixteen ideas for plugins that lean on the graph went into `docs/demo-coverage.md` (three new proposed tools, the Process miner, Response tracker and Structure analyzer, and extensions to Whyfinder, Lint, Ripple and Crosswalk); four dimensions of a plugin (who is in the loop, acts as whom, where it runs, whose rules) with a profile table and the matrix grouped by the first; a *Partial* row, Reacting to changes, since the hub has no events or merge-time checks; and `docs/research/graph-questions.md`, 38 graph questions by shape as input for a path read's ADR. A raw query route stays declined (ADR-0062 decision 6). Earlier: Amos asked whether a local Armature could act as a remote's clone in a Git analogy, so that local-first plugins share one workspace instead of each building Quiver's sync. The answer is ADR-0065 (Proposed) and `docs/workspace-hub-plan.md`: a *workspace hub* is the same API from the same code in a second mode on one person's machine, serving tools on loopback; a workspace is a branch created on the remote and cloned from its export; a push replays each local commit through the remote's `/api/v1` with `If-Match`, a local-to-remote commit table rewriting `asOf` and two message trailers keeping the local id and time; branches, merges and `User` registration stay on the remote (400 `remote_only`). TerminusDB's own clone and push were rejected, since they skip the remote's pipeline. The store underneath is TerminusDB or a file-backed store behind an adapter interface extracted first; the file-backed store is required for a designer release, because TerminusDB has no native Windows build, and a TerminusDB maintainer confirmed on Discord that one is doable but not on their roadmap and would need funding this project does not have (PR #66 records it). Nothing is built; ADR-0065 waits on Amos with eleven questions in the plan's §16.

### Done: The workspace hub planned (2026-10-28, PRs #64 to #66)

- **ADR-0065, Proposed (PR #64):** two roles for a hub (remote and workspace); a workspace a branch created on the remote and cloned by `GET /export`, `GET /schema` and `main`'s users; the workspace hub refuses branch creation, merge, branch deletion and user registration (400 `remote_only`) and runs the whole pipeline locally; push by replay over `/api/v1`, one batch write per local commit with `If-Match`, the commit table rewriting `asOf` (exact because a workspace has one writer and replay starts from the base), trailers `Armature-Workspace-Commit` and `Armature-Committed-At` in ADR-0025 decision 2's reserved form; delivery the remote's merge with a schema-generic review and the invariants run over every merged state; the owner only, on loopback; agent writes refused until the remote can authenticate an agent's push. Rejected: the store's clone and push, a library per tool, an unrestricted peer. On acceptance it annotates ADR-0025, ADR-0032, ADR-0055 and ADR-0059
- **`docs/workspace-hub-plan.md` (PRs #64, #65):** the cycle route by route; what a clone lacks (state older than its base, which constraints 13 and 16 and attestation staleness read; the subjects at each referenced `asOf` recommended, forwarding when online); capabilities by role; three install paths (a `workspace` compose profile, a desktop program with TerminusDB beside it, one over files) and four container runtimes by license (Colima and Podman Desktop the free defaults); what the hub asks of a store (no WOQL, a versioned document store, ids minted for nine Hash-keyed classes) with Dolt and Neo4j compared (Neo4j not recommended); the file-backed store's layout (remote snapshots, one commit file per local commit holding full states, which is the push's body); Hash ids (check AI) and the diff route's JSON patch as formats any other store must reproduce; seven steps, step 1 the adapter interface; eleven questions in §16
- **PR #66:** the TerminusDB maintainer's answer recorded in §10
- `.claude/CLAUDE.md` lists the plan; the ADR index lists ADR-0065; no route, schema or client-facing change, so no `client-changes.md` entry

### Done: Graph-native plugins and kinds of plugin (2026-10-28, PR #67)

- **`docs/demo-coverage.md`, graph-native tools:** the Process miner (the commit graph as a record of design process, backward design measured, one record's decision timeline; closes no per-person aggregates), the Response tracker (design changes after each administration's results) and the Structure analyzer (single points of failure, load-bearing objectives, hidden modules, structural duplicates; closes impact); Whyfinder, Lint, Ripple and Crosswalk extended; trust decay a proposed Design Graph lens. A section says what the graph adds at this scale (paths, relationships with reasons, graph × time, algorithms; time the distinctive one), names a **generic path read** as an ADR candidate, and keeps traversal in the hub so the file-backed store loses no read
- **Kinds of plugin:** four dimensions; where a plugin runs follows from identity (under the `header` resolver every client sits beside the hub; anywhere waits for OIDC; the link to a hub is a credential, not an installation); a profile table for all twenty tools; the matrix's columns grouped interactive, agent, batch, gate (no gate built or planned); **Reacting to changes** as a *Partial* row, since a gate can only poll `changes?since=` or run inside the client. Totals over 24 capabilities
- **`docs/research/graph-questions.md`:** 38 questions by shape (fixed paths, closures, something missing, field conditions, across time, algorithms, and three per-person questions that must be refused as negative tests); which existing reads answer which; a path read covers four shapes, history and the export cover time and algorithms; two ways to offer paths, declared as configuration or sent in the request in a small schema-checked grammar that refuses per-person tallies by construction
- The interactive matrix artifact still shows 2026-10-27; no route, schema or client-facing change

### Facts from 2026-10-28 worth carrying forward:

- **TerminusDB ships no release binaries** (v12.0.6 to v12.1.0-rc1 have no assets); the Snap is at v12.0.8 (12 October 2026); there is no Homebrew formula; Windows is WSL or Docker only. The build's `qsave_program` with `stand_alone(true)` and `foreign(save)` makes one executable whose macOS library links (Homebrew's SWI-Prolog and GMP) are untested, check AH. TerminusDB is Apache-2.0
- **A native Windows TerminusDB existed:** `terminusdb-labs/terminusdb-desktop` (2020, 3.0.0) bundled a Windows server binary and a Linux AppImage into Electron. A maintainer said on 2026-10-28 that Windows is doable but may need SWI-Prolog fixes coordinated with its maintainer under a consultancy contract, signing is deferred, desktop builds are not on their roadmap, and bringing them forward would need funding; Amos declined a meeting, having no budget. An app that bundles TerminusDB signs it inside its own bundle, so an unsigned portable build would suffice
- **Docker Desktop's terms of August 2026** exclude use connected to a university or research institution from the free personal tier; OrbStack is free for personal non-commercial use only; Colima and Podman Desktop are free for everyone
- **The adapter is a concrete class** imported by 23 modules; the invariants engine and the intelligence reads use `getDocument`, `getDocuments`, `queryDocuments`, `allDocuments`, `getCommit` and `at`. Nothing under `app/lib/api/` sends WOQL. The document API ignores commit metadata (check V4), which is why the workspace hub's trailers go in the message. The diff route's format is TerminusDB's JSON patch, unnamed in `docs/api.md`

### Facts from 2026-10-27 worth carrying forward:

- **MCP's 2026-07-28 revision is stateless:** no `initialize` handshake, the version on every request, `server/discover`, sampling deprecated, Tasks an extension; the v2 TypeScript SDK (`@modelcontextprotocol/server`) needs Node 20 and Zod 4, which `@armature/types` already uses (4.3.6), and serves 2025-era clients statelessly by default
- **stdio MCP servers take credentials from the environment** and do not follow the specification's OAuth flow; an HTTP server must not pass the assistant's token through to another service
- **Storyline's SCORM interaction ids are positional** (`Scene3_Slide3_MultiResponse_0_0`) and cannot be set in Storyline; reordering changes them, renaming does not. Variables are reported only through custom xAPI statements, and Articulate does not support adding files to published output
- **Canvas passes only a score from a SCORM package;** question-level results need an LRS
- **DataShop's web services** (documented as of 2018) import a KC model into a dataset and read learning-curve points and student-step records; the official client is Java only

## 2026-10-26, evening

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 8 with the second client planned: Quiver, a local-first item manager (2026-10-26 evening; `mossglenn/quiver`, private, its plan at `docs/plan.md` there; nothing changed in this repository; PR #60 still open, CI green and mergeable).** Amos asked for a plan for a tool that imports and exports question banks, edits items part by part, aligns them and assembles formative assessments, local-first as a real test of branching and merging. Planned against a survey of comparable tools and formats: a workspace is a folder of JSON files bound to a branch `<user>/<name>` of a hub, checked out from `GET /export` at a commit; local commits with reasons pushed as batch writes with `If-Match`; pull and deliver as the hub's merge in each direction, `main` receiving only clean merges; a fragment-by-fragment merge review over the hub's per-field conflict report, resolved by check Z's base-restore in three honest commits; ten scripted scenarios with two users in two workspaces on one machine; eight milestones, Tauri 2. Found on the way: **the merge route applies the store's merge but never runs the invariants engine over the merged state**, so two clean branches can merge into `duplicate_sequence` or a placement approved under a retired item; that is Quiver's first ask (its plan §8) and plan §6's merge-policy evidence, filed as issues here when Quiver's milestone 5 produces the scenarios. **Nothing in What's Next is triggered** beyond that; OIDC gains Quiver's two-machine case as a second trigger.

### Done: Quiver planned: the local-first item manager, the second client (2026-10-26 evening, `mossglenn/quiver`)

- **The plan** (`~/Dev/quiver/docs/plan.md`, commit `4103c4c` on its `main`, private on GitHub): purpose (a bank owner's tool, and the first client that must merge), what comparable tools settled (Moodle's versions and statuses, Canvas's lack of them, Inspera, OpenOlat's review, TAO; GIFT, Aiken, Moodle XML, a QTI 2.1 and 3.0 profile, text2qti; Anki, Dolt, Figma and Patchwork on conflicts), the workspace-on-a-branch model, features by surface, Tauri 2 with a folder of JSON files, ten branch-and-merge scenarios, what crosses and what stays, the asks tested against a second client, eight milestones, the ten decisions
- **The sync model rides on what the hub already has:** `POST /branches` at check-out, `GET /export?ref=` as the clone, batch writes with `If-Match` as the push, the merge route in the workspace's direction as the pull and toward `main` as delivery, `changes?since=` to refresh, `armature.mergeSource` to make the branch deletable. Conflict resolution uses check Z (restore the conflicting documents to base on the workspace branch, merge, then commit the resolution); an `InsertConflict` is resolved by rewriting to `main`'s content, pending a lettered check of whether identical inserts conflict at all
- **Found:** the merge route does not run the invariants engine over the merged state (`branches.ts`, `apply` then the witnesses, no `check`); the plan's scenarios 7 and 8 show it. Also noted: the store's list diff makes any two-sided edit of `options` one conflict, which is why the fragment-level view belongs in the client
- **Absorbed:** the text2qti exporter plugin (plan §8, 2026-10-20) is Quiver's milestone 4; the DQ mapping's "not in the hub" fields (authored difficulty, solutions, tags, license) are shown as such in Quiver's editor, the evidence plan §6 asked for from an imported bank
- Nothing changed in this repository; the memory note `quiver-repo-and-plan` points at the new repository

### Facts from 2026-10-26 worth carrying forward:

- **macOS `nc -z -w 5` does not time out a connection attempt,** so a port a firewall drops hangs; `nc -z -G 5` does (Linux's `-w` is fine)
- **A Droplet size is not offered in every region at every moment;** `doctl compute size list -o json` gives each size's regions
- **Playwright's `extraHTTPHeaders` on a browser context adds a header to every request, in every tab,** which makes the forged-identity check a real-browser test rather than a script
- **The workspace hoists `@playwright/test` to the repository root's `node_modules`;** a script outside the repository resolves it with `require.resolve('@playwright/test', { paths: ['<repo>/app'] })`
- **CoQui's proxy answers its redirects with a relative `Location`,** so behind Caddy there is no `http://` hop

PR #56 gave the seed the design's response to the pilot (a finding citing the 38% metric, then a second bias item), so "adequate when?" reads `PartiallyAssessed` then and `FullyAssessed` now, and the Walkthrough's step 3 says which placements the cohort met. PR #57 made `@armature/client` installable from another repository (the types a peer dependency) with a CI job that installs it on CoQui's stack. PR #58 lets a client run beside an instance: the network named `armature`, Caddy importing `docker/sites/*.caddy`, `load_schema.js --recreate` to reset a demonstration, rehearsed on a fresh VM with a stand-in client. The CoQui demo on a Droplet (a change in CoQui seen in the viewer, on the seed) now waits on CoQui's side; a handoff note is in CoQui's `docs/`. 285 Vitest tests in 21 files, 17 browser tests. **Nothing in What's Next is triggered.**

### Done: The hub made ready for a client on its server (2026-10-25, PRs #55 to #58)

- **Dropped connections (PR #55):** the store answers `Connection: Keep-Alive` with no `Keep-Alive` timeout and closes an idle connection 2002 ms after its answer (check AG, measured on a raw socket); Node's fetch keeps one for 4 s, so a request can go out on a connection the store is closing (`UND_ERR_SOCKET` "other side closed"). Reproduced once in 110 sends at an idle gap of 1993.6 ms (0 of 71 at whole milliseconds from 2030). `request()` in `store.ts` sends a read once more after 50 ms on `UND_ERR_SOCKET` or `ECONNRESET` from `fetch` or the body read: a GET, a POST with `X-HTTP-Method-Override: GET`, or the diff (`read: true`); never a write, which the store may have committed. `store.test.ts`, 8 tests over a stubbed fetch (6 fail on the old adapter). Plan §9 "Connections" records it and names a keep-alive timeout under 2 s (needs `undici`) as the step that would cover writes. Also: the history-memo test cleaned up through the branches route, which refuses a branch with unmerged commits, so every run since PR #54 left a `test-history-*` branch; it now deletes through the adapter
- **The local reseed (What's Next item 6):** the database deleted and recreated, `main` from 455 commits (408 test commits) to the seed's; `--clear-instances` would have kept the log
- **The design's response to the pilot (PR #56):** two seed commits after the results: the designer's `DesignFinding/bias-objective-rests-on-one-item` on the bias item, `regarding` its objective, `evidence` the 38% metric, `asOf` the results commit, `Open`; then `AssessmentItem/output-bias-mc` ("Telling output bias from training data bias", Approved, Analyze) placed in the capstone at sequence 4, and the finding replaced as `Addressed` with its resolution. *Distinguish Bias Types* reads `PartiallyAssessed`, 1 item, at the dataset's `asOf` and `FullyAssessed`, 2 items, now. 69 documents. The seed's counts moved in the tests (alignment and item-statistics scopes, the drawing 39 records and 27 junction edges, the changes lens naming two commits)
- **The Walkthrough's step 3 (PR #56):** the trace describes the assessment as it stands, so step 3 counted the post-pilot placement among those the cohort met; `completeWalk` restates it when the changes read shows placements inserted since `asOf` ("4 items are placed … today, 3 of them approved. 1 was placed after the cohort's commit, so the cohort met 3"). The Narrative 1 figure re-exported from a fresh seed at the same composition
- **The client installable elsewhere (PR #57):** `pnpm add github:mossglenn/armature#path:packages/client` failed with `ERR_PNPM_FETCH_404`, since `@armature/types@0.1.0` resolves only in the workspace; the types are now a peer dependency (and a dev dependency for the workspace). `packages/client/install-check/run.sh` packs both, installs the tarballs with pnpm outside the workspace and runs a stub-backed consumer under `tsc` with a Next app's options, Vitest, `tsx` and a Next 16 build with `transpilePackages`; CI's new `client` job runs it (about 25 s); checked to fail on the old manifest, without `transpilePackages` and on a type error. - Each merge after Amos's "confirm and merge"; CI green on `main` at `9a35100`. 285 Vitest tests in 21 files, 17 browser tests, five CI jobs

### Facts from 2026-10-25 worth carrying forward:

- **The store closes an idle keep-alive connection about two seconds after its answer and names no timeout** (check AG); undici keeps an idle connection 4 s when told none, so a reused connection can be closed under a request. Undici usually sees the close first: the window is a fraction of a millisecond, found only by sweeping the idle gap in 0.2 ms steps (1 in 110)
- **`--clear-instances` empties the data in a new commit and keeps the log; only deleting the database restores a fast store** (`load_schema.js --recreate`). A test run adds commits to `main` (user registrations and their cleanup), so a long-lived local store slows again
- **A history entry names its commit `commit`, not `id`** (the hub's history route); a Hash-keyed id (placements, attestations, declarations) is the same after a reseed, a commit id never is
- **TypeScript source in `node_modules`:** Next (Turbopack) fails with "Unknown module type" without `transpilePackages`; Node refuses type stripping there (`ERR_UNSUPPORTED_NODE_MODULES_TYPE_STRIPPING`); Vitest and `tsx` compile it; `tsc` checks it (`skipLibCheck` covers declaration files only)
- **pnpm resolves a git-installed package's dependency spec against the registry**, so a workspace-only dependency fails outside the workspace; packing the packages to tarballs and installing those reproduces it, which is what the install check does. npm cannot install from a repository's subdirectory at all
- **In a shell script, `echo "$(cmd)"` hides `cmd`'s failure from `set -e`, and pnpm's `--silent` hides its errors**; found while proving the install check fails where it should
- **Caddy 2.10's `import` of a glob matching no file is a warning, not an error;** a site file's host-matched routes merge into the same `:80` server as the instance's catch-all
- **A container on another compose project's external network comes back after a reboot** (Docker restores the network first); start the client after the instance on a first bring-up, which creates the network
- **`namedCommits` follows the export's document order:** with several commits named, pick one by its label, not by index (the finding's results commit came first)
- **Seed changes ripple:** the trace describes the head, so walk text claiming "what the cohort met" needs the changes or `asOf` read; tests asserted counts of records, junction edges, scopes and legends

PR #53 made edges tellable apart: columns ordered by their real edges (the anchor edges weigh 0.01; the seed's crossings between neighboring columns 24 to 8), edges attached at ports (`lib/graph/ports.ts`, one per edge per side, ordered by the other end's height), resting edges at 60% opacity and solid when lit. PR #54 made selecting a record answer at once: the pages stream the record's reads to the view (first byte 0.34 s instead of 5.0 s on a store with 440 commits), and the hub keeps a history answer by the head it was read at (`lib/api/memo.ts`; the store walks the whole log for a document touched rarely, 4.7 s for 20 entries); history responses now carry the ETag the contract promised. 274 Vitest tests in 20 files, 20 browser tests. **What's Next item 3 is now triggered** (the socket drop recurred locally); the rest still wait for a client or evidence.

### Done: The Design Graph's rendering at every zoom, and selection answering at once (2026-10-23, PRs #52 to #54)

- **The canvas (PR #52):** the light theme's canvas and cards were both white; the canvas is zinc-50 (`--graph-canvas`), the edge labels' backgrounds follow it, dark keeps its black. Figure mode's white is set under `.graph-figure`, not `.light`: React Flow puts its `colorMode` on the container as a class, so the live canvas is `.react-flow.light` and a value under `.light` overrode the tint (found when Amos saw no change)
- **The dot grid at three scales (PR #52):** `app/lib/graph/grid.ts`: gaps 24, 96 and 384 canvas units; a layer is fully drawn while its spacing on screen is between 18 and 72 px (a ratio of 4, so exactly one layer is full at every zoom from 0.15 to 2), fading in over the 4 px below and out over the 16 above; every dot keeps its size on screen (1, 1.25, 1.5 px) by dividing the canvas-unit size by the zoom; each layer's `offset` cancels React Flow's half-gap tile shift (the dot sits at the corner of a tile moved back by `gap / 2`), so coarser dots fall on finer ones as major marks, checked in the browser against the drawn patterns; the finest layer zinc-300, the coarser two zinc-200 (dark zinc-800 and zinc-900), after Amos found zinc-400 noisy. `GraphGrid` in `GraphView.tsx` renders one `Background` per layer; figure mode draws none. 9 unit tests across the zoom range in steps of 0.01, a browser test at the extremes
- **Column headers that fit (PR #52):** a name wraps to two lines at its best break above its rule while that fits the column's pitch (its width plus the gap, less a gutter); below that a short form from `stages.ts` (`{ header, short }`; "Assess. lessons" wraps too, keeping both ideas) chosen by `app/lib/graph/headers.ts`, widths estimated from five glyph classes fitted to measurements (within a tenth, wide for 22 of 23 names); the band is 44 px and the whole-graph fits take React Flow 12.12's per-side padding (52 px at the top). Per column, so a 1280 px window's fit mixes forms. Below about zoom 0.25 the short forms clip; drawing the rule alone there was judged unnecessary. 6 unit tests, a browser test zooming out and back
- **Columns ordered by their real edges (PR #53):** the anchor edges that pin a column weigh 0.01 in dagre's ordering instead of 1 (their `minlen` still pins); on the seed the crossings between neighboring columns fall from 24 to 8 and the height from 1776 to 1406. A seed test bounds the crossings at 12; the height is not asserted, since dagre's result depends on the order the store returns documents in (1640 in CI's fresh seed)
- **Ports (PR #53):** `app/lib/graph/ports.ts` gives each node one source port per outgoing edge on its right and one target port per incoming on its left, spaced evenly and ordered by where the other end sits; the node draws the handles (`portOffset`), each edge names its two. A node announces a changed port count with `useUpdateNodeInternals` only when it differs from the counts last announced and a tick later: the flow's store outlives the keyed flow, so after a hide the new flow first shows the old nodes and then syncs the new ones, and an announcement during that sync fired the queued fit on init off one node and left the view at zoom 1 (a mount flag was not enough: strict mode runs a mount effect twice). Checked in the browser: every edge starts and ends exactly 3 px from a handle's center (its half-width), before and after a hide. 4 unit tests
- **Resting edges at 60% opacity (PR #53):** still 1.25 px; a lit, selected, hovered or lens-marked edge and its arrowhead go solid
- **Streamed reads (PR #54):** `page.tsx` and the Focus page no longer await a selected record's attestations, history and trace; the promise is passed to `GraphView`, which remembers what landed with the promise it came from (no synchronous state set in an effect, which the hooks lint rule refuses), so the panel opens at once saying the history is on its way. The lens's reads and the Focus page's trace still wait, since they shape the drawing. Measured on the local store: first byte 0.34 s instead of 5.0 s, the URL changing 240 ms after a click
- **The history memo (PR #54):** `app/lib/api/memo.ts`, `CommitMemo`, a bounded in-process memo keyed by the commit and the read's parameters (a read at a commit is immutable, ADR-0025), with hit and miss counts; the history route reads the head before and after the store call and keeps the answer only when the head did not move, since the store's history answer names no commit. The route's ETag was set from that absent commit, so history responses carried none against api.md's rule; they now carry the head. A scratch-branch test checks the hit and a fresh read after a write; the plan's §9 records the measured cost (4.7 s for 20 entries, 1.8 s for 5, 0.28 s for 1, on 439 commits) and names a WOQL query over the commit graph as the next step
- **Tests:** 274 Vitest tests in 20 files (22 new), 20 browser tests (3 new); `visual-language.md` (surfaces, the grid, ports, the ordering, the opacity), `api.md` (history's ETag and memo), `development-plan.md` §9 and CLAUDE.md's module lists updated with each PR

### Facts from 2026-10-23 worth carrying forward:

- **React Flow puts its `colorMode` on the canvas container as a class** (`react-flow light`), so a stylesheet scope named `.light` applies to the live canvas, not only to figure mode; figure-only values go under `.graph-figure`
- **React Flow's dot grid is in canvas units,** gap and dot radius both scaled by the zoom (`size` is a diameter; radius 0.21 px at a fit zoom of 0.42). Each pattern tile starts at `translation % gap`, is moved back by `offset * zoom + gap / 2`, and holds its dot at `radius`, so two layers with different gaps land half a fine gap apart unless `offset` cancels it (`lib/graph/grid.ts` has the arithmetic). Several `Background`s stack with distinct `id`s
- **Dynamic handles and React Flow's fit on init:** `useUpdateNodeInternals` on mount disturbs the fit the `fitView` prop makes (it waits for the first measurement); a mount flag is not enough since Next's dev server runs effects twice under strict mode; and after a keyed remount the flow's store (in the provider outside) first shows the old nodes and then syncs the new ones, so an announcement during that sync fires the queued fit off one node and lands at zoom 1. Announce only a changed count, compared with the counts last announced, and a tick later
- **React Flow 12.12 takes per-side fit padding** (`{ top: '52px', right: '8%', ... }`), used to keep the header band clear; edge paths start 3 px from a handle's center (its half-width)
- **A promise passed from a Server Component to a Client Component streams:** the response stays open until it settles and the client receives the value; `instanceof Promise` holds on the client. Remember what landed with the promise it came from rather than setting state synchronously in an effect (the hooks lint rule refuses that)
- **The store's `/api/history` cost is proportional to the commits walked:** it walks the log until it has `count` commits that touched the document, so a document touched rarely costs the whole log per page (4.7 s for 20, 1.8 s for 5, 0.28 s for 1, on 439 commits; the log lists 500 in 0.7 s). Its answer carries no data-version header, so the hub's history route had no ETag until 2026-10-23
- **dagre's output depends on the order nodes and edges are added,** which follows the order the store returns documents in; a fresh seed (CI) and a store with history differ (height 1640 against 1406), so a layout test asserts crossings, not dimensions. The anchor edges' `weight` governs the ordering; their `minlen` pins the rank regardless
- **A measured text width in the browser calibrates a pure estimate:** uppercase 11 px with tracking 0.05em is about 8.1 px a letter, 8.8 for C D G O Q U, 10.6 for M W, 7.0 for T J, 3.6 for I, a space or a period; the table in `headers.test.ts` is the calibration
- **Playwright's default viewport is 1280×720,** narrower than a screenshot at 1700, so a test written from a wide screenshot can assert a form the test viewport never shows; `locator.or()` accepts either. A test needing a zoom control checks `isEnabled()` first, since the control disables at the limit
- **The browser suite reuses a running dev server locally** (`reuseExistingServer`), so a test runs against `next dev` with strict mode, which CI's `next start` does not have; a mount-effect bug can show locally only
- **A full Vitest run can fail several files at once on a store stall** and pass on a rerun; run the failing files alone before suspecting the change
- **Auto mode declines `gh pr merge`** ("Merge Without Review"); the user's explicit "confirm and merge" is what lets it through, after `gh pr view --json mergeable,mergeStateStatus,statusCheckRollup` confirms the state

### Facts from the 2026-10-22 night worth carrying forward:

- **html-to-image 1.11 deep-clones an `<svg>` and inlines computed styles on it alone**, not on its children, so any stroke or fill a stylesheet gives a path is lost and React Flow's edges vanish from the picture. `exportPng` inlines `stroke`, `fill`, `opacity`, fonts and the rest onto `svg *` first. `getComputedStyle` of an element inside `<defs>` reads `none` for stroke and fill (nothing is rendered there), so the arrowheads are colored explicitly, not from computed style
- **Forcing light under a system theme without a toggle:** `@custom-variant dark { @media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { &:where(:not(.light, .light *)) { @slot; } } }` in Tailwind v4 makes every `dark:` utility stop at a `.light` element; hand-written dark rules take `:where(:not(.light *))`; the canvas's colors are custom properties the `.light` block resets. React Flow's `colorMode` is set to `light` in figure mode as well
- **An effect's first-run flag must be set before any early return**, or the first real change is treated as the first run; the symptom was a selection by click whose history never loaded while the same selection from the URL worked, which the earlier browser tests did not cover (they selected by URL or read export-held data)
- **Playwright and a bezier edge:** `locator.hover()` aims at the bounding-box center, which is rarely on the stroke, so React Flow's edge `mouseenter` is tested with `dispatchEvent('mouseover')` on the edge's path (React derives enter from over/out); a node's `hover()` is fine. A test's `toHaveText` on a legend heading must match the rendered case (`Lens · coverage`), not the CSS uppercase
- **Hunk-level commits without `git add -p`:** intermediate versions of a file written to the working tree, typechecked, staged and committed in turn, the final version restored last; `git rebase --onto <amended> <old-first> <branch>` folds a fix into an unpushed branch's first commit when `-i` is unavailable
- **`npm install` before pinning records the caret in the lockfile's workspace entry** (`"html-to-image": "^1.11.13"` while package.json said `1.11.13`); the background security review caught it; regenerate with `npm install` after pinning
- **The stamp's date is the reader's local date** (`getFullYear`/`getMonth`/`getDate`), not UTC's `toISOString`, which was a day ahead at 21:44 local
- **The lens legend's counts are declarations, not objectives:** an objective declared by two modules is one node with the weaker verdict but counts twice in the legend, matching the Coverage View; the note under the legend says so
- **`gh run list --branch main` lists `pages-build-deployment` first**; `--workflow CI` picks the right run; `gh run watch --exit-status` blocks until it ends (about two and a half minutes)
