- 235 Vitest tests and 10 browser tests; CI green on `main` at `d1a83d6`; SESSION.md closed with the evening's decisions and facts

### Facts from the 2026-10-22 evening worth carrying forward:

- **dagre cannot pin a rank**: its rankers bound a rank from below (`minlen`) and then shorten edges, so a node whose edges all go right drifts right (an objective landed in the items' column). The columns come from an anchor chain that bounds every node from both sides: one anchor per stage plus a terminal one, chained at `minlen: 2`, an edge from a stage's anchor to each node and from each node to the next anchor; `ranksep` is halved since the anchors take ranks. Same-stage edges (prerequisites) are drawn but withheld from the ranking (`app/lib/graph/layout.ts`)
- **React Flow's `colorMode="system"` stayed `light` under a dark scheme in the production build**; the view reads `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` itself and passes `dark` or `light`. Its stylesheet is imported at the top of `globals.css`, before the overrides, so they win on equal specificity (a component import loads after). Its UMD build expects a `jsxRuntime` global that React 18's UMD lacks (the prototype shimmed it with `createElement`)
- **No `window` in render**: a client component renders on the server too; links built during render use `usePathname()`; `window` belongs in effects and handlers. An `<li>` inside an `<li>` is restructured by the browser's parser and surfaces as React error #418 (hydration); the browser tests' "no console error" rule catches both
- **A hub read for a selected record is routed, a view-only change is `replaceState`** (`lib/graph/state.ts` `SERVER_KEYS`); on the Focus page the root is the implicit selection so the first load makes no extra round trip. `next start` warns that it does not work with `output: standalone` but serves the pages (CI and the Playwright config use it; the viewer image runs the standalone server)
- **The trace route looks the record up before judging its type**: a missing record of any class is a 404; `untraceable_type` (400) needs a real record, so `focus.test.ts` asks with one seed document per class. The hub's traceable set is nine types; a `Lesson` is not among them
- **A palette is a property of the column order**: the adjacent-pair checks are over neighboring columns, so reordering columns re-opens the palette. Two shades of one family pass in neighboring columns only when they differ sharply in lightness (the dark theme's band is 0.48 to 0.67). The enumeration script lived in the scratchpad; the method (the dataviz skill's validator, `validate(palette, { mode, surface })`, a report of `[name, ok, message]` rows) is in `docs/visual-language.md`
- **Regenerating the glyphs**: `V=$(npm view lucide-static version); for pair in evidence:file-text need:target objective:flag group:layers activity:play item:circle-help assessment:clipboard-check module:box course:book-open dataset:table metric:gauge note:pencil finding:triangle-alert junction:link; do curl -sf "https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/lucide-static@$V/icons/${pair##*:}.svg" -o "$D/${pair%%:*}.svg"; done`, then the inner markup of each `<svg>` into `app/lib/graph/glyphs.ts` (the key `group` is now `lesson`)
- **The commit `timestamp` is seconds since the epoch as a number** on history entries and branch heads; the client's type said string until 2026-10-22. The seed's history shows the `--clear-instances` reload as commits ("Clear instance data", "Load Armature schema") touching every document
- **CI runner facts**: `gh run rerun --failed` reruns one job; the Actions API (`gh api repos/.../actions/jobs/<id> --jq '.steps[]'`) gives per-step timings when `gh run view` does not; `grep stall` matches "Install"
- **Playwright locators**: a heading regex that matches both the page title and the panel's `h3` is a strict-mode violation (use `level: 1`); `getByRole('button', { name: 'Trace' })` also matched a node named "Trace one training step" (use `exact: true`); a `Link` click raced the 5-second URL check once, so the test asserts the `href` and navigates to it

### Facts from the 2026-10-22 afternoon worth carrying forward:

- **A path parameter takes the bare id:** `/intelligence/module-content/Module/x` is an extra segment and a 404, as for `coverage/:moduleId`; the client strips the `Module/` prefix (`localPart`); query parameters (`?module=`) accept either spelling

- **`gh pr checks` right after `gh pr create`** prints "no checks reported" for a few seconds; poll until rows appear, then `--watch`

**Where things stood at the close.** **Phase 7 done except a standing hosted instance, which is deferred by decision (2026-10-22, PRs #37 to #42, all merged, CI green on `main` at `308e151`).** Amos decided not to keep a hosted instance running until a plugin needs async access to a hub; the deployment is verified and `deployment.md` stands it up again. The day's work: CI job timeouts (#37); ADR-0062 accepted and built, the operator tools `check`, `delete` and `put` in `app/lib/operator/`, bundled into the hub image, with an adapter fix for a deleted document's id (#38); every operator commit marked in its metadata, `check` over the seed in CI, and the tools shipping in the hub image, never the client package (#39); the seed's content gaps filled and the trace fixed for activities placed through a group (#40); `GET /api/v1/intelligence/item-statistics` with the Coverage View as ADR-0058's first client, revision `2026-10-22` (#41); "Separate API service" marked resolved in plan §6 (#42). **Most ready next: the ordered module-content view** (What's Next item 1).

### Done: Phase 7 follow-ups: operator tools, the seed's gaps, item statistics (2026-10-22, PRs #37 to #42)

- **CI job timeouts (PR #37):** `timeout-minutes` 10 for `app` and `images`, 15 for `contract`, chosen from the last 20 runs (healthy 20 to 95 seconds a job; during npm's incident one run passed in 680 seconds, the next stalled). A timed-out job reports `cancelled`
- **ADR-0062 accepted and built (PR #38):** `app/lib/operator/check.ts` runs the invariants engine over a whole branch (every document the batch, every id "replacing"), skipping `attester_not_caller` and `subject_in_same_write` by code; `repair.ts` has `delete` (a document, or `--scratch-branch` for `test-`, `warmup-`, `anon-`, `future-`, `operator-`), and `put` through `writeDocuments` with `--unchecked` passing the new `invariants: 'skip'` (no route passes it; a test reads every API module). The CLI is `app/operator.ts`, bundled by `npm run build:operator` into `dist/operator.mjs`, which the hub image carries (`docker compose exec hub node operator.mjs check`; locally `npm run operator --workspace app -- check`). Bundled, not a plain script: Node's type stripping refuses `errors.ts`'s parameter properties and the extensionless imports. The operator must be a registered `User`; a reason is required
- **The AC4 adapter fix (PR #38):** the store answers a GET of a deleted document with HTTP 200 and the 404 as text in the body; the adapter returned it as the document, so `findExisting` "found" it and a write reusing the id was a `type_conflict` forever. `request()` now raises the embedded status. Plan §9's AC4 line corrected; the route's 404 had been right by accident (the string has no `@type`)
- **The operator commit marker and the seed check (PR #39):** every repair runs on a transient `operator-…` branch cut at the target's head and is applied onto the target as one commit with `{ armature: { operator: { tool, rulesSkipped } } }` (check AF); a target that moved meanwhile is refused, not merged; the transient branch is always deleted. The marker shows in the log and on `GET /branches/:name`'s head, not in `/api/history` (V3c), so the message keeps its "rules were skipped" note. CI's contract job runs `check` after seeding. Decided: the tools ship in the hub image, never in `@armature/client` (ADR-0062's last open item)
- **The seed's content gaps (PR #40):** `revise-prompt-mc` makes Module 2's Write Effective Prompts FullyAssessed (2); Module 1 has the Practice `sort-ai-systems` (sequence 1) and the group `how-a-model-learns` (sequence 2) with a video and a worked example; the Draft `training-loop-mc` assesses nothing and is a formative check in the worked example; `User/demo-reviewer` (Partner College) raises the finding and attests, and every other record whose class carries `createdBy` names the designer (filled from `schema.json`'s classes). 61 documents in the first commit, 66 in all. The export tests now require two pseudonyms and both institutions
- **The trace through a group (PR #40):** `moduleContext` found an activity's module only through `ModuleActivityLink`; it now follows `ActivityGroupMember` to the group's `ModuleActivityGroupLink`. Same edge shape, no client-changes entry
- **The item-statistics read (PR #41):** `GET /api/v1/intelligence/item-statistics?module=&unit=&branch=|ref=`: each item (bank or a module's placements) with every `LearningMetric` whose `subject` it is, newest administration first, each with the dataset's cohort and administration date; `units` in scope before the filter; `itemsWithoutStatistics`. No "latest" policy (ADR-0058's open question). Capability `armature/intelligence.item-statistics`, revision `2026-10-22`, `client-changes.md` entry, `@armature/client`'s `intelligence.itemStatistics()` with `CLIENT_REVISION` 2026-10-22. The Coverage View shows every administration under each item, read at the coverage's commit. 193 tests
- **Plan §6 (PR #42):** "Separate API service" marked resolved (done 2026-10-21, ADR-0026 decision 4)

### Session notes: 2026-10-22 (Phase 7 follow-ups: CI timeouts, ADR-0062's operator tools with the commit marker, the seed's gaps, the item-statistics read; PRs #37 to #42)

- Confirmed PR #36 merged and green; Amos decided not to keep a hosted instance until a plugin needs async access; added CI job timeouts from measured durations (PR #37)
- Built ADR-0062 (accepted): `check`, `delete`, `put` bundled into the hub image; building `delete` exposed the AC4 adapter bug, fixed; then marked every operator commit in metadata through a transient branch and `apply` (new check AF), put `check` over the seed in CI, and decided the tools never ship in `@armature/client` (PRs #38, #39)
- Filled the seed's gaps with Amos's four content choices (FullyAssessed in Module 2, Module 1's activities with a group and a formative use, an unaligned draft, two people in `createdBy`); the richer seed exposed the trace's missing group hop, fixed (PR #40)
- Built `GET /api/v1/intelligence/item-statistics` as grouped observations with no "latest" policy, the Coverage View as its first client, revision `2026-10-22` and the client function (PR #41); marked "Separate API service" resolved in plan §6 (PR #42)
- 193 tests in 8 files; CI green on `main` after every merge; the ordered module-content view is the most ready next item

### Facts from 2026-10-22 worth carrying forward:

- **Node cannot import the API's modules directly:** strip-only type stripping refuses `errors.ts`'s parameter properties (`ERR_UNSUPPORTED_TYPESCRIPT_SYNTAX`) and the relative imports have no extensions. Anything that reuses the API outside Vitest or Next is bundled with esbuild, as `build:hub` and `build:operator` are
- **Check AC4 was never handled until 2026-10-22:** a GET of a deleted document is HTTP 200 with `Status: 404`, headers and the JSON as text; `Store.request()` now raises it. Any new store call that can meet a deleted document gets this for free
- **Check AF:** `apply` from a branch cut at a target's head (`origin` a commit) carries an insert or a delete onto the target as one commit with `commit_info.metadata`, parent the previous head; the transient commit stays readable by id on no branch; an empty diff answers 200 and makes no commit. Metadata shows in `/api/log` and a branch head, never in `/api/history` (V3c)
- **The trace reaches an activity's module through a group** since PR #40; the alignment read's module scope still counts only assessment placements for unaligned items
- **The seed fills `createdBy` from `schema.json`** (`carriesCreatedBy`, inheritance followed), so a new class that carries it is covered without a list; the finding names the reviewer explicitly
- **Constraint 3 meets stale fixtures:** a test that places activities in `Module/how-ai-works` must use sequences other than 1 and 2 (the seed's), or it gets `duplicate_sequence`
- **`gh pr merge --rebase --delete-branch` fast-forwards local `main` itself** when run from the branch; a following `git pull --ff-only` can print "Cannot fast-forward to multiple branches" harmlessly. Confirm with `git rev-parse --short HEAD` and `origin/main` separately (`git rev-parse a b` with `--short` errors)
- **A zsh function named `head` shadows the `head` command** in the same script (as `g` is an alias); name helpers distinctly
- **Two units of work began on `main` by mistake today** (nothing committed there); `git switch -c` carried the changes over. Create the branch before the first edit
- **The CI contract job's `check` step** builds the operator bundle (`npm run operator --workspace app -- check`) from the repository root with the job's `TERMINUS_*` variables; it takes about a second

### Done: Phase 7, the hosted instance: the server chosen, the deployment rehearsed and run (2026-10-21 night, PR #35; PR #36 open)

- **The server:** a small virtual private server running `docker/docker-compose.yml` as is, a DigitalOcean Droplet (`s-2vcpu-4gb`, $24 a month). Rejected: Railway, Render and Fly.io (each translates the compose file into per-service configuration, so `deployment.md` would stop describing the hosted instance); DigitalOcean App Platform (no persistent volumes, so the store loses its data on every deploy; the $0 tier is static sites only); Vercel for the viewer alone (the hub would have to be public). Oracle's always-free ARM tier noted as the only $0 standing option, with capacity and idle-reclamation caveats. Measured on this Mac: 375 MiB running, a 1.0 GB `next build` process
- **The rehearsal and its fixes (PR #35):** a fresh Ubuntu 24.04 VM (Lima 2.2.1, 2 virtual CPUs, 4 GB, no mounts, no port forwarding, reached at its own address as the internet would) following `deployment.md` from a clean copy. Three gaps fixed: `restart: unless-stopped` on every service (nothing came back after a power-cycle before); the `operator` service (`docker compose run --rm operator node load_schema.js`, node:22-alpine on the private network, the checkout read-only, dependencies in a volume via `docker/operator-entrypoint.sh`) because a fresh server has no Node and Ubuntu packages Node 18; Caddy (`caddy:2.10.2-alpine`, `docker/Caddyfile`, profile `public`, ports 80 and 443, HTTPS for `ARMATURE_SITE`, proxying the viewer only) with the viewer's port moved to loopback. Verified from outside: 80 served the viewer, 3000/3100/6363 refused, `/api/v1` through Caddy the viewer's 404, back after a power-cycle with data intact; Docker's published ports bypass `ufw` (a probe on 8080 was reachable); a Cloudflare quick tunnel served the viewer publicly over HTTPS
- **The Droplet run (documented in PR #36):** `nyc3`, created and removed with `doctl` and a scoped token kept in the macOS Keychain; a cloud firewall allowing 22, 80, 443 (UDP 443 for HTTP/3); an `armature` user in the `docker` group; steps 1 to 3 from a clone of `main`. Let's Encrypt issued for `armature.amosglenn.com` on the first attempt; HTTPS over HTTP/2; `http://` 308 to `https://`; 3000/3100/6363 timed out from outside (dropped by the provider's firewall); back 26 seconds after a reboot with the stored certificate; build 104 seconds, peak 1.67 GB. One more gap: settings passed inline would be lost on a later `up` (the hub recreated with the development password, Caddy with `localhost`), so they go in `docker/.env` (mode 600, gitignored); with it an `up` recreated nothing. Destroyed by choice afterward with its firewall; the GoDaddy `A` record is Amos's to remove

### Session notes: 2026-10-21, night (Phase 7: the hosting decision, a rehearsal on a fresh VM, a run on a DigitalOcean Droplet; PR #35, PR #36 open)

- Laid out the hosting options with prices from the hosts' own pages and measured memory; Amos chose a small VPS running the compose file as is (DigitalOcean), after ruling out App Platform (no persistent volumes) and asking for a free way to test first
- Rehearsed `deployment.md` on a fresh Ubuntu 24.04 VM under Lima; found and fixed no restart policy, Node needed on the server (the `operator` service), and no TLS (Caddy, the viewer on loopback); verified from outside, through a reboot and over a Cloudflare tunnel; Docker bypasses `ufw` (PR #35, merged, CI green on `main` at `5616a44`)
- Ran it on a Droplet in `nyc3` with a scoped token from the Keychain: Let's Encrypt for `armature.amosglenn.com` on the first attempt, the hub's and store's ports dropped by the cloud firewall, back 26 seconds after a reboot; found that settings belong in `docker/.env`; destroyed it with its firewall at Amos's choice
- Documented the `.env` fix and the DigitalOcean recipe (PR #36); its contract job stalled twice in `npm ci` during npm's incident and was cancelled, to be re-run and merged next session
- Saved two memories: the DigitalOcean token's Keychain location and use, and `ssh` being an iTerm2 function in Amos's shell

### Facts from the 2026-10-21 night worth carrying forward:

- **`ssh` in Amos's shell is an iTerm2 function** (osascript opens a tab); scripted remote commands call `/usr/bin/ssh`. The DigitalOcean token is in the Keychain (`digitalocean-armature`), read inline into `DIGITALOCEAN_ACCESS_TOKEN` per command, never `doctl auth init`; the account's SSH key is `AmosGlennKey` (id 60277929); `amosglenn.com` DNS is at GoDaddy. Both are in auto-memory
- **Docker bypasses `ufw`:** a port published on every interface is reachable whatever `ufw` allows (verified with a probe container). Loopback bindings and the provider's firewall are the gates
- **A read-only bind cannot take a nested mount whose mount point does not exist:** `..:/repo:ro` plus a volume at `/repo/scripts/node_modules` failed on a fresh clone (no such directory) and would have passed on this Mac (it exists). The operator service mounts its volume at `/deps` and links `/node_modules`, which Node's resolution reaches from `/repo/scripts`
- **`sg docker -c` runs `sh`,** so a bash `time ( … )` fails; put the commands in a script file. A user added to `docker` needs a new login; Lima reuses its SSH session, so `sg docker` stands in
- **Cloudflare quick tunnels:** one attempt failed to register over QUIC on IPv6; `--protocol http2 --edge-ip-version 4` worked. Querying a new name before it exists caches "not found" locally; resolve through `dig @1.1.1.1` and `curl --resolve` to check
- **zsh does not word-split `$VAR`,** so `curl $R` with `R="--resolve h:443:ip"` passes one argument; quote the option and its value separately
- **DNS for a new instance:** an `A` record (a CNAME needs a name; a Droplet has only an address); check it at the domain's own nameserver (`dig @ns17.domaincontrol.com`) before starting Caddy; Let's Encrypt resolves independently, so a public resolver's cached absence does not affect issuance
- **`gh run list` on `main` includes `pages-build-deployment`;** filter by `workflowName` CI when confirming a merge
- **Lima VM recipe:** `limactl start --name=<n> --tty=false --cpus=2 --memory=4 --disk=40 --mount-none --containerd=none --network=vzNAT --set '.portForwards=[{"guestPortRange":[1,65535],"guestIP":"0.0.0.0","ignore":true},{"guestPortRange":[1,65535],"ignore":true}]' template:ubuntu-24.04`; the VM's address on `lima0` is reachable from the Mac; its home is `/home/<user>.guest`

### Done: Phase 7, the rest: the hub as a process and container, the schema guide for tool authors, the viewer as a client, an instance as three containers, the terms (2026-10-21, PRs #32 to #34)

- **The hub as its own process (PR #32):** `app/lib/hub.ts` (`startHub({ port, hostname })` over `@hono/node-server`, port 0 for a free one) and `app/server.ts` (reads `.env.local`, `PORT` default 3100, `HOST` default loopback, SIGTERM). `npm run build:hub` bundles the entry, the API, the workspace packages, hono and zod into one 751KB `dist/server.mjs` (esbuild 0.28.2, exact); `start:hub` runs it. The build step exists for the packages' extensionless TypeScript imports, which Node's loader refuses, not for the `@/` alias, which had already gone from `app/lib/api/`. `docker/hub.Dockerfile` (two stages, 166MB, health check on `/discover`); `lib/hub.test.ts` compares the socket's answers with `app.request()` (same `/discover` body, same commit in a list's ETag, a write with identity and a 412 on a stale `If-Match`, a 404 by code, a clean stop). CI bundles it and builds and starts the image. Two automated security reviews flagged the hub's exposure under the `header` resolver; the host-run hub and the compose publication both bind loopback by default
- **The schema guide for tool authors (PR #33):** `docs/schema-guide.md` in three parts (a hub and the client; the graph, the conceptual core kept; the work of a tool: reading, writing, every refusal by code, CoQui's eight mappings as the worked example, examples through the client, export, runtime discovery, when the schema does not fit, things never to do). Every example ran against the seed in a throwaway test; a batch accepts a forward reference by literal id in either order, contrary to what was first written
- **The viewer as a client (PR #34):** the pages read a hub over HTTP through `@armature/client` via `app/lib/viewer.ts` (`ARMATURE_HUB_URL`, default `http://localhost:3100`; `ARMATURE_HUB_PUBLIC_URL` for the footers' raw-data links, omitted when the hub is private); the catch-all route and `@hono/vercel` deleted; `next.config.ts` transpiles the client and emits Next's standalone output. Verified with `.env.local` moved aside. The client's intelligence summaries were typed as `@id`/`@type` documents while the hub returns `{ id, type, label, … }`: `Summary`, `CoverageItem`, `CoverageDeclaration` added, the Coverage View uses them
- **An instance as three containers (PR #34):** `docker/viewer.Dockerfile` (standalone output with `outputFileTracingRoot` at the workspace root); the compose `hub` profile runs store, hub and viewer on one private network, the viewer public on 3000, the hub on loopback 3100 (`ARMATURE_HUB_BIND`), the store on loopback 6363 (`TERMINUSDB_BIND`; it had been on every interface with the development password). Verified together: the viewer rendered the course from the hub by service name; hub and store refused the LAN address. CI builds and starts both images
- **The terms and the deployment guide (PR #34):** `docs/deployment.md` (the terms, what runs and what each container publishes to whom, who can do what and why, the five steps, several projects, what is not there yet); the Terms block in CLAUDE.md and the explainer's glossary (a server is a machine; a store is one database and the TerminusDB process holds many; a host is one Node process; a hub is one host bound to one store; a container adds nothing). Every "writes must stay on the local machine" passage now says the hub stays on its server's loopback with only the viewer published. Tests 175 in 7 files

### Session notes: 2026-10-21, afternoon and evening (Phase 7: the hub as a process and container, the schema guide for tool authors, the viewer as a client, an instance as three containers, the terms; PRs #32 to #34)

- Served the hub as its own process (`app/server.ts`, `app/lib/hub.ts`), bundled it with esbuild into one file, built `docker/hub.Dockerfile` and the compose `hub` profile, tested the socket against `app.request()`, put the bundle and the image in CI (PR #32); found the `@/` alias already gone from `app/lib/api/`, the bundle step needed for the packages' extensionless imports; two security reviews led to loopback defaults
- Rewrote `docs/schema-guide.md` for tool authors in three parts, ran every example against the seed, corrected the batch forward-reference claim (PR #33)
- Laid out hosting options with pros and cons; the discussion exposed that the Next.js app was a second hub, and settled the vocabulary (server, store, API, host, hub, instance, client, viewer)
- Made the viewer a client of the hub through `@armature/client`, removed the catch-all route and `@hono/vercel`, added `docker/viewer.Dockerfile`, ran an instance as three containers with only the viewer public (the store's port moved to loopback), fixed the client's summary types, wrote `docs/deployment.md`, added the Terms block, and aligned every document with the three-container plan (PR #34)
- 175 tests in 7 files; CI green on `main` after each merge; the hosted instance is the one Phase 7 item left

### Facts from the 2026-10-21 afternoon and evening worth carrying forward:

- **Next's standalone output in a workspace:** with `outputFileTracingRoot` at the repository root, `.next/standalone` keeps the layout (`app/server.js` beside a traced `node_modules`); `.next/static` and `public` are not traced and are copied beside the server in the image. 58MB; the viewer image runs `node app/server.js` with `HOSTNAME=0.0.0.0`
- **Deleting a route file leaves a stale `.next/types/validator.ts`** that makes `tsc` fail until the next `next build` (or `rm -rf .next`); it is not a code error
- **A forward reference by literal id inside one batch is accepted** in either order (the rules check the branch as it will be after the write); `@capture`/`@ref` are needed only for a junction whose id is derived. The schema guide says so because a test showed it
- **The LAN-refusal check:** `curl http://$(ipconfig getifaddr en0):<port>/` answering `000` is the evidence a port is bound to loopback; `docker compose config` shows `host_ip: 127.0.0.1` on a published port
- **esbuild under npm's install-scripts policy:** the postinstall is blocked with a warning, but the binary comes from the optional platform package and works; `npm ci --ignore-scripts` in the Dockerfiles for the same reason
- **Compose profiles:** `profiles: [hub]` keeps `docker compose up -d` store-only; `--profile hub` adds the hub and viewer; `docker compose --profile hub rm -sf hub viewer` removes them and leaves the store. A port-binding change recreates the store container; the named volume keeps its data
- **A `trap … EXIT` inside one Bash tool call fires when that call's shell exits**, which restored `.env.local` between two calls and made the next call's `mv` fail; move a file aside and restore it in the same call, or not at all
- **The client's intelligence types had drifted** from the hub's summaries (`{ id, type, … }`, not `@id`/`@type`); a page typed against them caught it. A report shape a client types should have a test reading the typed fields
- **Hosting options** (2026-10-21): a small VPS running the compose file as is, Railway, Render, Fly.io, Vercel for the viewer alone; third-party price summaries disagree, check the host's own page. Not chosen

## 2026-10-21

### Done: Phase 7, first four pieces: the contract suite in CI, the two packages, the version contract (2026-10-21, PRs #27 to #31)

- **The contract suite in CI (PR #27):** a `contract` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` starts `terminusdb/terminusdb-server:v12.0.7` as a service container, waits for it (`scripts/wait_for_store.js`, no dependencies), loads the schema, seeds, and runs `npm test`; `tsc --noEmit` joined the lint job. `app/vitest.global-setup.mts` waits until the seeded database answers a read of `main`, then warms the store with the tests' kind of work (a branch, a write through the pipeline, a coverage read, the branch deleted through the adapter) before any test's clock starts; `vitest.env.ts` reads `.env.local` for the setup and the workers
- **Files in sequence (PR #29):** `fileParallelism: false`. Every branch's commit lands in the database's one commit graph, so parallel files contended; on the runner the store answered `Transaction retry count exceeded in internal operation` (500, after 14 s) once on `main`, on a commit that had passed on its PR minutes earlier. 49 s locally instead of 31, 1 m 17 s on the runner
- **`@armature/types` (PR #28):** the generated `types.ts` and `schemas.ts` moved unchanged (history kept, `check:types` in sync) into `packages/types`, exporting `.` and `./schemas`; the repository became an npm workspace (`package.json` at the root: `app`, `packages/*`; one lockfile; `npm install` at the root); `scripts/` deliberately outside so the `terminusdb` client is resolvable only from `scripts/node_modules` (ADR-0055). TypeScript source, no build; Next compiles it through `transpilePackages`; installable by git reference (pnpm `#path:packages/types`). `generate:types` and `check:types` run from the root
- **`@armature/client` (PR #30):** `packages/client`, one function per route in the reference's five groups; `{ data, commit, version }` results (the commit from the `ETag`); `{ branch }`/`{ ref }` on reads; `message`, `branch`, `ifMatch` → `If-Match` on writes; `Armature-User` from `user`; `HubError` with the stable code and the body's details; takes a `fetch`, so `app/lib/api/client.test.ts` runs every route over `app.request` in-process (the app dev-depends on the client for that). `ClassMap` added to the generator so reads are typed by class name
- **ADR-0059 accepted and built (PR #31):** `app/lib/api/version.ts` holds `REVISIONS` (the dated entries of `docs/client-changes.md`, newest first; a test checks they agree), `CAPABILITIES` (each mapped to its route prefix; a test walks `app.routes` and fails on a route under none), `discovery()` and `negotiate`, the middleware on every request; `routes/discover.ts` serves `GET /discover` and `GET /schema?branch=|ref=` (ADR-0027 decision 4, scheduled in Phase 1, built here); every response, errors included, carries `Armature-Version`. The open question decided: a newer revision is served on a read and refused on a write. The client sends `CLIENT_REVISION` on every call, returns `version`, takes `capabilities`, adds `discover()` and `schema()`. api.md gained the conventions, a Discovery section and two error codes; the explainer §14.9; three `client-changes.md` entries dated 2026-10-21

### Session notes: 2026-10-21 (Phase 7: the contract suite in CI, the workspace and two packages, ADR-0059; PRs #27 to #31)

- Put the 145-test suite in CI against a TerminusDB service container (PR #27), with `tsc` in the lint job and a Vitest global setup that waits for the seeded database; reproduced the 2026-10-20 cold-store failures by relaunching Docker Desktop (cold VM, not cold container: one stalled request, then a shared-branch cascade) and made the setup do real work first; one cold cycle then passed
- Made the repository an npm workspace and moved the generated types into `@armature/types` (PR #28), `scripts/` kept outside; Next created a stray root `tsconfig.json` once, which broke the build until deleted
- CI on `main` then failed once on `Transaction retry count exceeded`: parallel test files contend on the database's one commit graph; files now run one at a time (PR #29)
- Built `@armature/client` (PR #30): one typed function per route, `{ data, commit, version }`, `HubError`, fetch injection, `ClassMap` in the generator, 14 contract tests over `app.request`; chose the contract test's home (`app/lib/api/`, app dev-depends on the client)
- Explained the three answers to a newer revision; the user chose serving reads and refusing writes; accepted and built ADR-0059 (PR #31): `negotiate` middleware, `GET /discover`, `GET /schema` (ADR-0027 decision 4 at last), two guard tests, the client sending its revision; 169 tests, CI green on `main` after each merge

### Facts from 2026-10-21 worth carrying forward:

- **Working in the workspace:** `npm install` once at the root links `@armature/types` and `@armature/client` into `node_modules/@armature/`; `npm run typecheck` covers all three packages; `npm run lint` lints only the app. Adding a package means a folder under `packages/` with a `package.json`, then `npm install` at the root; the app dev-depends on a package whose contract test lives in `app/lib/api/`. An import of a `.mts` helper fails `tsc` under this tsconfig; use a `.ts` helper and an extensionless import. `git mv -k` exits 0 without moving an untracked file
- **The one-off root `tsconfig.json`:** the first `next dev` after the workspace install created `tsconfig.json` at the repository root (Next's template, no `@/` paths) and the next `next build` read it and failed with module-not-found for every `@/` import. Deleted; two later dev runs and a build did not recreate it. If it recurs, set `turbopack.root` in `app/next.config.ts`; do not commit the file
- **The store contends across branches:** every branch's commit goes into the database's single commit graph, so concurrent writers to different branches still contend for one transaction, and the store gives up with `Transaction retry count exceeded in internal operation` (500). The suite runs files one at a time because of it; a plugin with concurrent writers would see the same 500 and should retry. Worth a lettered check in `scripts/platform_checks.js` and a plan §9 line when a plugin needs it
- **A cold Docker VM, not a cold container**, is what stalls the store; see Blockers
- **Hono's `app.routes`** lists every registered route with its full path (basePath included) and method; middleware registered with `use` appears with method `ALL`. The capability-coverage test filters those out
- **A test's `commit` must come from a route that sets an `ETag`:** `GET /branches` sets none, so `(await hub.branches.list()).commit` is undefined; `documents.list` sets one
- **`btoa` and `fetch` are available in the Vitest global setup** (Node 22+), so the setup needs no `node:` imports beyond `fs` for `.env.local`
- **Hash-keyed placements in a 422 test need real targets:** a `reference_class` violation for a missing target also answers 422, so a test for `duplicate_sequence` must name documents that exist (`Assessment/mod1-assessment`, `AssessmentItem/distinguish-ai-mc`, `hallucination-mc`); the seed's ids come from `id("Type", "local")` calls, so grep for those, not for quoted ids
- **Edit tool, same anchor twice:** when one edit already replaced a passage, a second edit with the old text fails; it is not a sign the file is wrong
- **`gh pr merge --rebase` on a branch behind `main`:** rebase locally first (`git rebase main`, then `git push --force-with-lease`); uncommitted work survives a `git stash -u` around it. A chained commit script that fails at one `git add` (a path already removed from the index) lets the later `git add -A` sweep everything into the last commit; `git reset --soft` to the base and recommit fixes it before the PR is reviewed

### Done: The dashboard question and ADR-0062 (2026-10-20, PR #23)

- Asked whether an Armature dashboard (graph visualizer, query runner, raw editor) lives inside the API wall or is a plugin: a first-party client in `app/app/`, on the plugin side of the boundary, like the existing pages; split along the identity line (actions as a `User` are a client, actions as the operator are inside the wall); the visualizer's whole-graph read is ADR-0031's export; no raw query route (ADR-0021, ADR-0055)
- Asked how an admin fixes what the rules will not let a client fix: **ADR-0062 Proposed**, operator repair is a commit outside the plugin API. Every repair is a new commit (the store's delete is one, AC2; the deleted document stays readable at the commit before, AC3); the store's enforcement is the floor (a referenced document cannot be deleted, AC1); no superuser mode or bypass header; three tools in order (`check`, the invariants engine over a whole branch read-only; `delete`; `put --unchecked`), none built; scratch branches deletable when named as such; `User` edits an operator task until a client needs a route. Plan §8 declines a raw query route and a superuser mode. Annotates ADRs 0024 and 0025. Platform check AC placed on the Phase 6 branch, as the ADR says

### Later 2026-10-20 facts worth carrying forward:

- A weakening schema change (a minimum removed) and an additive one (a new class) both load with `node scripts/load_schema.js` and no `--clear-instances`; existing documents conform. Run the loader from the repository root after `set -a; . app/.env.local; set +a` so it reaches the same database as the app
- The store returns no field for an empty Set. A test or reader that does `doc.assesses.includes(...)` crashes on an unaligned item; use `idsIn` or `?? []`. The generated interface still types the field as `string[]` (the generator's convention for every Set without a minimum, as `evidence` already was)
- Tests in one scratch-branch `describe` share the branch, so a count asserted as a literal (items in scope) breaks when an earlier test in the block adds one; read the figure before the write and compare
- A Hash-keyed junction in a batch write with `@capture` needs every required field; `ItemInstance` requires `pointValue` and `randomize`, which a 400 names by path
- The explainer's rules table runs to 17 (20 since ADR-0063). When a constraint is added, that table, CLAUDE.md, `api.md`'s rules table and the invariants index comment all change
- Impact's `within` is generic (`otherReferences` on the junction), so a new junction appears in impact with its container named without any change; the trace needs a case per hop
- The store decorates every `Set` it returns with `@max_cardinality: "inf"` and refuses to load it back (AE5); `exporter.normalizeSchema` drops it, and `GET /schema` returns the normalized form. A GET of a missing document is HTTP 200 with a `Status: 404` line and a JSON 404 envelope in the body (AC4). Re-import of exported documents with their nested subdocument ids succeeds, and the store regenerates the nested ids (AE7)
- The seed's documents carry no `createdBy` (written outside the API), so a test of `createdBy` rewriting needs documents written through the API; the seed's attestation has `attestedBy`

- `tsc` is in CI since 2026-10-21 (`npm run typecheck`); before that a lint-clean change broke it once
- Zsh in this environment: `g` is an alias, so a shell function named `g` fails to parse; `vitest` with relative `--config` and `--root` from the repo root fails to resolve, absolute paths work
- Web Crypto (`crypto.subtle`) is available in the Hono routes under Node and Vitest, so the export's HMAC needs no `node:crypto` import and stays host-neutral

## 2026-10-20

### Done: Precedents survey decisions (2026-10-20, PR #19)

- **ADR-0059 Proposed:** capability negotiation per request. Adopts the capabilities half of research candidate 0039 and drops its LSP-style handshake, since the API is stateless HTTP with no session to hold a negotiated result. Modeled on the Model Context Protocol's 2026-07-28 revision: a request may name the dated contract revision it was written against (`Armature-Version`) and the hub capabilities it relies on (`Armature-Capabilities`); the hub answers each request alone, 400 `unsupported_version` or `unsupported_capability` listing what it serves; `GET /api/v1/discover` optional; identifiers `<prefix>/<name>` where the prefix names the *definer* (`armature`, or a reverse domain name) and a capability is always something a hub does; the declaration flows one way, client to hub. Shape changes stay a new prefix (ADR-0026 decision 2); candidate 0039's projections and cadence halves stay open. Numbered 0059 per the index's rule (next number past the candidate block, candidate named in Status). Built with Phase 7's typed client or on the first trigger it names; one question left for acceptance (a revision newer than the hub's: refuse or serve). Annotated two-way in ADR-0026, the index, the candidates file, the plan's ADR queue and one sentence in `api.md`. Nothing built; no client-changes entry
- **text2qti is the first exporter target** (plan §8): v0.8.0, Markdown-based text to a QTI 1.2 package Canvas imports. Chosen over Storyline because Canvas is an LMS the outcomes importer can read back from (one LMS closes Narrative 1), the syntax read in reverse models a plain-text importer that assigns `fragmentId` on parse, and it reaches Canvas without writing QTI. Plugin work outside this repository; what the export loses is evidence for ADR-0031
- **`NeedEvidenceLink.confidence` is reliability, not direction** (plan §6 "Evidence that argues against a need", schema guide, explainer, ADR-0009 decision 5 annotated): the link's existence is read as support; evidence found to argue against a need has no relation, and a negative confidence would conflate the axes. Wait for usage per ADR-0010; the path is an optional `relation: Supports | Challenges` beside `confidence`, read as `Supports` when absent. Precedent: Micropublications
- The survey's three checkboxes marked decided; SESSION.md brought current from the git history at the start of the session (it had been one session behind)

## 2026-10-18, Phase 5, the client changelog and the research mappings

### Done: Phase 5: findings and attestations (2026-10-18, PR #16)

- **Platform checks AA and AB:** a Hash key may include Optional fields, absent fields are not confused with each other, a second insert is `DocumentIdAlreadyExists`, and `PUT create=true` with no `@id` replaces in place (AA). The store's diff of a list is positional; a no-op replace is a commit and regenerates subdocument ids; comparing fragments by `fragmentId` at two commits reports exactly the changed fragment, ignores a reorder, and still works after `apply`, which leaves the branch commit out of the target's history (AB1 to AB7)
- **ADR-0028 Accepted and built:** `Attestation`, a relationship record inheriting `DesignRecord`, keyed by Hash over `subject`, `fragmentId`, `claimRef` and `attestedBy`: one current verdict per person, part and claim, no client `@id`. `verdict: AttestationVerdict` (`Affirmed`, `Declined`), optional `reason`. **The claim is optional** (the plan had `claimText` required): with one the verdict is about the claim, without one it is an endorsement. `attestedBy` and `asOf` are Optional in the schema and always filled by the hub. `DesignNote` and `DesignFinding` gain an optional `fragmentId`. ADRs 0012, 0020, 0023, 0025, 0056 and 0057 annotated; ADR-0057 decision 5 amended for attestations (a replace refills `asOf`). The schema loaded without clearing data
- **Pipeline (`write.ts`):** `attestedBy` filled before the replace lookup, since it is in the key; the Hash-key lookup sends required key fields to the store and matches Optional ones in the hub (a template on an Optional reference is a 500, Y2); an attestation's replace refills `asOf`; its subject may not be written in the same request (422 `subject_in_same_write`, added to ADR-0028 decision 5 while building)
- **Constraints 14 to 16** (`invariants/attestation.ts`, `invariants/fragmentRef.ts`; `fragments.ts` holds a record's parts by `fragmentId` and the canonical comparison form): `claim_incomplete`, `attester_not_caller`, `fragment_needs_one_subject`, `unknown_fragment` (the fragment looked up through the schema's `fragment` category, at `asOf` when that is checked, else as the write leaves it)
- **Read:** `GET /api/v1/intelligence/attestations/:type/:id` at a ref compares the judged part at `asOf` and at the ref by `fragmentId`, never walking history, so it holds after a merge; states `Current`, `Stale`, `FragmentRemoved`, `Unanchored`. The trace carries attestations on the nodes it reports. No attester parameter, no per-person count (ADR-0021); `attestedBy` is refused as a list filter automatically
- **Seed:** one attestation in a second commit whose `asOf` names the first (48 documents, then the attestation)
- **Exit criterion met** (`intelligence.test.ts`): attestations made on a round branch, merged into a second branch, reported `Stale` there after option b changes, `Current` for option a, `FragmentRemoved` for a dropped option, and all `Current` on the round branch and at the attested commit. 126 tests
- Propagated to CLAUDE.md (constraints 13 to 16, the `Attestation` row, ADR-0028 in the reference list), PROJECT_CONTEXT, `api.md`, the schema guide, the explainer, README, the plan (Phase 5 all checked), the CoQui handoff and the ADR index

Linked from the README map, the handoff banner, plan §7 and CLAUDE.md; `prompts/update-session.md` gained step 5, which checks it at session close
- **Three mappings under `docs/research/`**, each read as text at a named commit, no code run: `dq-questionbank-mapping.md` (DQ QuestionBank Core, question-set 1.1; gaps named as evidence for ADRs 0030, 0031, 0033, 0034, candidate 0035 and ADR-0028's open `regarding`; rule 1 flagged), `tessera-mapping.md` (both directions, design to course and outcomes to design; join-key hazards an outcomes importer meets; a build must stamp its source commit; `ItemType` to `cmi.interaction` as evidence for ADR-0034), `quiqui-mapping.md` (a live audience response tool, AGPL-3.0 so interoperation through its YAML only; a session file built from a module's items at a commit; the formative-items gap)

## 2026-10-18, post-Phase 4 decisions and fixes

### Done: Post-Phase 4 decisions and fixes (2026-10-18, PRs #12 to #15)

- **ADR-0057 Accepted and built (PR #12):** `asOf: Optional<xsd:string>` on `LearningDataset`, `LearningMetric`, `DesignFinding`. The pipeline (`write.ts` step 3, `fillAsOf`) fills it on create with the head the write starts from (the `If-Match` commit when sent), keeps it on replace, and copies a dataset's value onto a derived metric. Decision 5, added at acceptance, records those three answers and that a commit cannot name itself, so a filled `asOf` predates a subject created in the same write (accepted when filled, refused when supplied). **Constraint 13** (`invariants/asOf.ts`): `unknown_commit`, `absent_at_commit`, `as_of_disagrees`; the write context's `asOfFilled` set holds the values taken from the head, which are not checked. Trace summaries carry `asOf`; Narrative 1 reads coverage at it
- **Branch-route race (PR #13):** a branch deleted between the list read and a log read made the store answer `api:UnresolvableAbsoluteDescriptor` (400), which failed `GET /branches` and the delete's holder search for every other branch. Both now skip the vanished branch (`gone()` in `routes/branches.ts`). Deleting an already-gone branch answers `api:BranchDoesNotExistError`, which was a 500; the unknown-ref set moved into the adapter as `isUnknownRef()` and gained it
- **List filters and the non-aggregation guard (PR #14):** the store also 500s on a template query over an Optional *enum* (checks Y4 to Y6 added: required enum filters, Optional enum 500, Optional string filters). The list route now applies required single-valued filters in the store and Optional or Set filters in the hub, paging after filtering. A filter on any field targeting `User` is refused, 400 `person_filter_refused` (ADR-0021 decision 3, pulled forward from Phase 5 because hub filtering would otherwise have made `?createdBy=` work); ADR-0021 annotated
- **ADR-0058 Accepted and built (PR #15):** item statistics are `LearningMetric`s whose `subject` (new, `Optional<DesignRecord>`) is the item, `derivedFrom` the administration's dataset and carrying its `asOf`; `AssessmentItem.difficultyIndex` and `discriminationIndex` removed (never written, so the schema loaded without clearing data). Constraint 13 checks a metric's `subject` at its `asOf`, and a value copied from a stored dataset is now checked too. The trace follows `subject` both ways; `?subject=` on the list route lists an item's metrics. ADR-0009 decision 1 superseded, ADR-0056's open question resolved
- **ADR-0007 confirmed (PR #15, docs only):** clients write `ModuleObjective` through the generic routes; the dated note gives the four reasons (the rule protected a field ADR-0056 removed; assigning is the write itself and inferring declarations from placements would erase `Uncovered`; two reference clients write them; Hash key, constraint 0 and attributed commits already guard them)
- Every change propagated to CLAUDE.md, PROJECT_CONTEXT, `api.md`, the schema guide, the explainer, the plan (§6 three questions resolved, Phase 5 guard checked, ADR queue 0057 and 0058) and the ADR index (35 ADRs). 109 tests; lint, `check:types`, `tsc` clean; platform checks all pass on v12.0.7

- Started Docker and the store; flagged three gaps in ADR-0057 (replace, same-write subjects, metric parents), agreed answers, accepted it with decision 5 and built `asOf` and constraint 13 (PR #12)
- Fixed the branch-route race with a `listBranches` spy that names a vanished branch, and found and fixed the 500 on deleting an already-gone branch (PR #13)
- Fixed the list route's filter 500, finding Optional enums fail too (checks Y4 to Y6), and built ADR-0021's non-aggregation guard with it, since hub filtering would otherwise have enabled `?createdBy=` (PR #14)
- Laid out options for item statistics and `ModuleObjective` writes; on the user's choice wrote and built ADR-0058 (statistics as `LearningMetric.subject`, the item fields removed) and confirmed ADR-0007's generic path (PR #15)
- Four PRs, 12 commits, each rebase-merged with CI green on `main`; 109 tests

## 2026-10-08, Phase 4

### Done: Phase 4: design intelligence (2026-10-08)

- **ADR-0029 (Accepted, decisions 1 to 3):** the coverage algorithm. A module's declaration is covered by the distinct `AssessmentItem`s placed in the module's assessments whose `assesses` names the objective, never the bank; two populations, Approved items with Approved placements and everything not Retired (ADR-0019 adopted and promoted); the default cut 0 `Uncovered`, 1 `PartiallyAssessed`, 2 to 4 `FullyAssessed`, 5 or more `OverAssessed`, provisional (P9). Decisions 4 to 6 (stored fields, write-time recompute, merge recompute) were built, weighed and superseded the same day
- **ADR-0056 (Accepted):** coverage is computed on read, never stored. The graph stores what people decided and what was observed; the intelligence reads derive the rest at a ref. `coverageStatus`, `projectedCoverageStatus` and the `CoverageStatus` enum left the schema; `ModuleObjective` is design intent only; the generators lost computed-field support; the write pipeline lost its coverage step; a merge is the store's `apply` and nothing else. Reversed ADR-0007's computed field, which PROJECT_CONTEXT had listed as settled, on the evidence of the machinery it required
- **ADR-0057 (Proposed):** records that refer to a state of the graph name its commit: an optional `asOf` on `LearningDataset`, `LearningMetric`, `DesignFinding` and Attestation, filled from the branch head when absent, validated as constraint 13 when supplied. Closes Narrative 1's "adequate when". Awaiting decision
- **Intelligence reads** in `app/lib/api/routes/intelligence.ts` over a per-request cached `Graph` (`intelligence/graph.ts`): `coverage/:moduleId` and `coverage?course=` (both figures with counts, thresholds as parameters, items behind each with eligibility, undeclared assessment), `alignment?module=` (items below an objective's level, objectives no item reaches at level, objectives without an activity, the unleveled), `trace/:type/:id` (the lifecycle walk evidence to metric both ways, hops naming field and junction, modules as context, notes and findings about what is reached), `impact/:type/:id` (every referencing document from `CLASS_FIELDS`, with the artifact each junction sits in). All at `?branch=|ref=` with the commit in `ETag`
- **Platform checks Y and Z.** Y: a template query filters on a required reference field; on an Optional or a Set reference field it is a 500 (`Graph.where` lists and filters). Z: a field conflict is cleared by restoring the base value on the target before `apply`; kept as a fact nothing now uses
- **Two 400 fixes:** each unrecognized key is reported at its own path; a client id on a Hash-keyed type is `bad_id`, not a 500
- **Placement key kept:** one placement per item per assessment (`ItemInstance`'s Hash key); two forms are two `Assessment`s; the reopening triggers are in the schema comment and plan §6
- **Coverage View:** `/` lists a branch's modules, `/coverage/<module>` renders the coverage read; both call the Hono app in-process. The app is titled Armature
- **Exit criterion met:** `intelligence.test.ts` walks Narrative 2 through coverage and alignment and Narrative 1 from a metric to the declaring module through trace. 95 tests; lint, types drift, `tsc` and `next build` clean

## 2026-10-08, Phase 3

### Done: Phase 3: generic writes and the invariants engine (2026-10-08)

- **ADR-0032 (Accepted):** identity resolution. A pluggable resolver chosen by `ARMATURE_IDENTITY` (`header`, carrying the caller's `externalId`; `oidc` named for later) yields claims; the hub resolves them to a `User` on `main`, the registry of record, by template query, registering one there on first encounter when the claims carry a `displayName`. Commit author and `createdBy` come from the resolved `User`, never the body: set on create, preserved on replace. A write on a branch that lacks the `User` carries `main`'s copy in the same commit; identical copies merge clean. `GET /api/v1/users`, `GET /api/v1/users/me`, `POST /api/v1/users` (main only, 409 `user_exists`). Agent users are `User` documents with an `agent:` `externalId`, registered by a person
- **ADR-0025 decision 6 amended:** `DELETE /api/v1/branches/:name` only when another branch holds the head (in its log or as a merge commit's `mergeSource`), never `main`, no force, because a deletion is the one mutation the store records nowhere. `If-Match` honored (412), head re-read before the delete (409 `branch_moved`)
- **Platform checks W and X** (all passing on v12.0.7): template query over HTTP via `POST` + `X-HTTP-Method-Override: GET`; identical inserts on two branches merge as an empty patch, differing ones are a 409 `InsertConflict` witness; a `PUT` list with `create=true` writes Hash-keyed documents without `@id`, honors `@capture`/`@ref`, returns the ids and fails as a whole; **`POST overwrite=true` merges values into a list rather than replacing**; `GET ids=[...]` batch-reads and drops missing ids
- **Generator:** `types.ts` now carries the schema as data (`CLASS_ANCESTORS`, `CLASS_KEY`, `CLASS_FIELDS`); a second output `app/lib/schemas.ts` holds strict Zod schemas per concrete class (`@min_cardinality` as `.min(n)`, enums from `VALID_*`, references as an id or `{ "@ref" }`, abstract subdocuments as discriminated unions, Set/List without a minimum optional). `check:types` covers both. `zod@4.3.6` is a direct dependency
- **One write pipeline** (`app/lib/api/write.ts`) behind `PUT /api/v1/documents/:type/:id` and the batch `POST /api/v1/documents`: Zod shape (400 `invalid_document`), what each document replaces (by `@id` or by Hash key fields), 409 `type_conflict`, `createdBy`, the invariants (422 `invariant_violation` with every violation), one `PUT create=true` over the list, `If-Match` as 412, afterWrite hooks. `GET /api/v1/documents/:type?field=value&count=&skip=` lists with filters
- **Invariants engine** in `app/lib/api/invariants/`: `index.ts` (context, registry, 422), `references.ts` (constraint 0, walking into fragments), `assessmentItem.ts` (8, 9, 10), `itemInstance.ts` (5, 10), `moduleContent.ts` (3, 4), `designFinding.ts` (11), `recompute.ts` (7, afterWrite stub naming affected modules; removed in Phase 4 by ADR-0056). Constraints 1 and 2 are Zod minimums; 6 falls out of constraint 0; 12 is the pipeline. The merge route reports the `InsertConflict` witness
- **Retired:** the eleven unversioned routes (eight GET lists, the coverage route, two POSTs), `lib/terminusdb.ts`, `lib/routeHelpers.ts`, `lib/validate.ts`; the `terminusdb` client left `app/` (scripts keeps its own). The catch-all is the only route file. A module's coverage is two list reads until Phase 4
- **Exit criterion met:** every CLAUDE.md constraint has a failing and a passing test in `app/lib/api/write.test.ts`; 68 tests across two files pass; lint, `check:types`, `tsc`, `next build` clean. The automated security review's two findings on the delete route (check-then-delete race, unbounded holder walk) were fixed in the PR

Built `identity.ts`, `classes.ts`, `routes/users.ts`, `CLASS_ANCESTORS` in the generator; the merge route learned the `InsertConflict` witness

## 2026-10-08, Phase 2

### Done: Phase 2: the version-control model (2026-10-08)

- **ADR-0055 (Accepted):** the API layer reaches TerminusDB only through one `fetch`-based adapter, `app/lib/api/store.ts`; the JavaScript client stays in `scripts/`. Decided on a reading of the installed client source: author welded to the connection user, a supplied data version kept in instance headers forever, errors flattened to strings, history `diff` option missing
- **ADR-0025 (Accepted):** design process data lives in the commit graph. No version fields ever (ADR-0010 amended); every write is a commit with the resolved `User` id as author and a required `message` in a JSON envelope; reads at `?branch=` or `?ref=`; `/api/v1` exposes bare commit ids as `ETag` and accepts `If-Match` (412 when stale), closing ADR-0054's open question; merge wraps `apply` three-way with a hub-computed base; no reset, squash or rebase exposed or called; interim `Armature-User` header until ADR-0032
- `scripts/platform_checks.js` checks M to V, all passing on v12.0.7, with corrections to the vendored docs: `apply` takes bare commit ids and reports a conflict as HTTP 409 with `@expected`/`@found` witnesses; a stale data version is HTTP 400; rebase runs X onto Y for `POST /api/rebase/X` with `rebase_from: Y`; branch DELETE needs a `{}` body; a read at a non-existent commit path is a 500, so commit ids are validated via `ValidCommit/<id>` in `local/_commits`; `Commit.parent` is single-valued and `apply` stores `commit_info.metadata`
- Routes, all Hono, all through the adapter: `GET /api/v1/documents/:type/:id?branch=|ref=`, provisional `PUT` (artifact and relationship classes only, until the invariants engine), `/history?branch=` with per-commit diffs, `/diff?from=&to=`, `GET/POST /api/v1/branches`, `GET /branches/:name`, `POST /branches/:name/merge` (409 `merge_conflict` with base, target and source values), `GET /branches/:name/changes?since=`
- Merge commits carry `{ armature: { mergeSource } }` in commit metadata because an `apply` commit has one parent and does not record the source it merged; the merge-base walk reads it from the log. First built as a `Merge-Source` message trailer, moved to metadata once check V showed the store keeps it
- `app.onError` maps typed `StoreError` by the server's `@type`; hub errors are `ApiError` with stable codes
- Seed gains `User/demo-designer` (48 documents); live store re-seeded
- Phase 2 exit criterion met by the walkthrough in `app/lib/api/app.test.ts` (25 tests, scratch branches, `main` untouched). `tsc`, lint, `check:types`, `next build` clean
- Security review of the interim header acknowledged: the provisional write cannot mint `User` documents; mutating routes stay local until ADR-0032

### Done: Types Generation

- `scripts/generate-types.js` — generates `app/lib/types.ts` and `app/lib/schemas.ts` from `schema/schema.json`; both **generated**, never hand-edited
- Two npm scripts in `app/package.json`: `generate:types` (write both) and `check:types` (drift check on both, runs in CI)
- Enums emit `VALID_*` const arrays + derived union types — arrays are runtime source of truth, and the Zod schemas use the same arrays
- Type mapping: xsd primitives → TS primitives, `sys:JSON` → `unknown`, `Optional<T>` → optional fields, `Set<T>`/`List<T>` → arrays, document Class references → `string` (@id), `@subdocument` references → the inline interface, enum refs → union types, inheritance as declared, property-less classes → type aliases
- Schema as data: `CLASS_CATEGORY`, `SUBDOCUMENT_CLASSES`, `CLASS_ANCESTORS`, `CLASS_KEY`, `CLASS_FIELDS` (every field, own and inherited, with kind, target type and cardinality). The hub keeps no second copy of the schema; a class with no category fails generation (ADR-0027)
- Zod mapping: strings non-empty and ≤ 10,000 chars, `@min_cardinality` → `.min(n)`, a Set/List without a minimum optional, references an id or `{ "@ref" }`, subdocuments inline with abstract ones as discriminated unions, strict objects
